L. 110-2 LAW n°2016-1087 of August 8, 2016 - art. 5 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf Laws and regulations organize everyone's right to a healthy environment. They help to ensure a harmonious balance between urban and rural areas as well as the preservation and sustainable use of ecological continuities. It is everyone's duty to ensure and contribute to the protection of the environment, including at night. Public and private persons must, in all their activities, comply with the same requirements.
L. 110-3 LAW n°2019-773 of July 24, 2019 - art. 21 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf In order to ensure the conservation and sustainable use of biodiversity, the national strategy for biodiversity, provided for in Article 6 of the Convention on Biological Diversity, adopted in Nairobi on May 22, 1992, is developed by the 'State in consultation with representatives of local authorities and their groups, socio-economic actors, in particular small and medium-sized businesses, and environmental protection organizations, in particular naturalist associations, as well as with members of the scientific community. The regions define and implement a regional strategy for biodiversity taking into account the orientations of the national strategy and developed under the same consultation conditions. Local authorities and their groups participate in the definition and implementation of this strategy at the level of their territory. The establishment mentioned in the article
L. 131-8 provides support to regions in developing their strategy and monitoring its implementation. The national strategy and regional strategies for biodiversity contribute to the integration of the objectives of conservation and sustainable use of biodiversity into public policies as well as to the coherence of the latter in these matters.
L. 110-4 LAW n°2023-580 of July 10, 2023 - art. 10 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I.-The State develops and implements, on the basis of available scientific data and in consultation with representatives of local authorities and their groups as well as other stakeholders, a national strategy for protected areas whose objective is to cover, through a coherent network of protected areas in mainland France and overseas, on land and at sea, at least 30% of the entire national territory and maritime areas under French sovereignty or jurisdiction. This network also aims to place at least 10% of the entire national territory and maritime areas under French sovereignty or jurisdiction under strong protection. The strategy mentioned in the first paragraph of this I aims at the protection of the environment and landscapes, the preservation and recovery of biodiversity, the prevention and mitigation of the effects of climate change as well as the valorization of the natural and cultural heritage of the territories. It takes into account the evolution of fire risk. This strategy is updated at least every ten years. The total surface area as well as the surface area under strong protection reached by the network of protected areas cannot be reduced between two updates. This strategy establishes the list of human and financial resources necessary to achieve the missions and objectives set out in this article. A decree specifies the definition and modalities of implementation of the strong protection mentioned in the first paragraph. II.-The State encourages the deployment of methods and projects that could result in the allocation of carbon credits under the “Low-Carbon” label in favor of protected areas and the actors contributing to their management. p.21 Environmental Code http://legifrance.gouv.fr/affichTexte.do?cidTexte=JORFTEXT000033016237 L. 110-5 LAW n°2021-1104 of August 22, 2021 - art. 60 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The French Republic reaffirms the primary importance of the contribution of overseas territories to its own characteristics, its environmental wealth, its biodiversity as well as its geostrategic base. State action contributes to the recognition, preservation and development of the biological, environmental and heritage riches of overseas territories.
L. 110-6 LAW n°2021-1104 of August 22, 2021 - art. 270 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf With a view to putting an end to the importation of raw materials and processed products whose production has contributed, directly or indirectly, to deforestation, forest degradation or the degradation of natural ecosystems outside the national territory, the State develops and implements a national strategy to combat imported deforestation, updated at least every five years. The national platform to combat imported deforestation set up as part of the strategy mentioned in the first paragraph aims to assist companies and public buyers in transforming their supply chains for the benefit of more sustainable, traceable and more materials. respectful of tropical forests and natural ecosystems, as well as the local communities and indigenous populations who live from them.

L. 120-1 LAW no. 2018-670 of July 30, 2018 - art. 4 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I. - Public participation in the development of public decisions having an impact on the environment is implemented with a view to: 1° Improving the quality of public decision-making and contributing to its democratic legitimacy; 2° To ensure the preservation of a healthy environment for current and future generations; 3° To raise awareness and educate the public about environmental protection; 4° To improve and diversify environmental information. II. - Participation confers the right for the public: 1° To access relevant information allowing their effective participation; 2° To request the implementation of a participation procedure under the conditions provided for in Chapter I; 3° To have reasonable time to formulate observations and proposals; 4° To be informed of the manner in which their observations and proposals were taken into account in the authorization or approval decision. III. - The prior consultation procedures organized in application of the town planning code respect the rights mentioned in 1°, 3° and 4° of II of this article. IV. - These provisions are exercised under the conditions provided for in this title. They apply with respect for the interests of national defense and public security and any secret protected by law. The course of public participation as well as the modalities of its conduct can be adapted accordingly. Chapter I: Public participation in the development of plans, programs and projects having an impact on the environment
L. 121-1 -A. Ordinance No. 2016-1060 of August 3, 2016 - art. 2 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf Chapter I of this title applies to public participation prior to the submission of the application for authorization of a project as defined in article
L. 122-1, or during the development phase of a plan or program as defined in article
L. 122-4, until the opening of the public inquiry or any other form of public participation provided for in Chapter III of this title. This prior participation concerns the procedures: 1° Of public debate and prior consultation falling within the competence of the National Commission for Public Debate in application of article
L. 121-8; 2° Prior consultation implemented by the project owner or the public person responsible for the plan or program in application of I of the article
L. 121-17; 3° Prior consultation carried out at the request of the competent authority to approve the plan or program or authorize the project in application of Article II
L. 121-17; 4° Prior consultation decided by the representative of the State following the right of initiative in application of Article III

L. 121-1 LAW n°2018-148 of March 2, 2018 - art. 2 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I.-The National Commission for Public Debate, an independent administrative authority, is responsible for ensuring respect for public participation in the process of developing development or equipment projects of national interest of the State, local authorities, public establishments and private individuals, falling within the categories of projects mentioned in article
L. 121-8, the list of which is established by decree in the Council of State, as long as they present strong socio-economic issues or have significant impacts on the environment or regional planning. The commission is also responsible for ensuring respect for public participation for the national level plans or programs mentioned in IV of the article.
L. 121-8. The National Commission for Public Debate may decide to organize a public debate or a prior consultation to discuss the opportunity, objectives and main characteristics of the project or the objectives and main orientations of the plan or program, socio-economic issues. economic issues attached to them as well as their significant impacts on the environment and land use planning. This debate or consultation makes it possible, where appropriate, to discuss alternative solutions, including, for a project, its lack of implementation. This debate or consultation also concerns the methods of information and participation of the public after its closure. Public participation is ensured throughout the development phase of a project, plan or program, from the initiation of preliminary studies to the opening of the public inquiry carried out in accordance with the provisions of Chapter III of this title or of Book I of the code of expropriation for reasons of public utility or, in the absence of a public inquiry, of the method of participation chosen. II.-The National Commission for Public Debate ensures compliance with good conditions of public information during the implementation phase of the projects referred to it until receipt of the equipment and works and, for the plans and programs mentioned in I, until their adoption or approval. It may, on its own initiative, or referred to it by a president of a particular public debate committee or by a guarantor mentioned in article
L. 121-1 -1, request the carrying out of technical studies or additional expertise. His decision is made public. At their request, it advises the competent authorities and any project owner or responsible public person on any question relating to public participation throughout the development of a plan, program or project. The National Commission for Public Debate also has the mission of issuing all opinions and recommendations of a general or methodological nature likely to promote and develop public participation. The National Commission for Public Debate and the specific commissions do not comment on the substance of the plans, programs or projects submitted to them.
L. 121-1 -1 LAW n°2018-148 of March 2, 2018 - art. 2 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I.-The National Commission for Public Debate establishes a national list of guarantors and makes it public. It can remove from its list any guarantor who has failed to fulfill its obligations. II.-The guarantor is designated from among the members of the list mentioned in I and compensated by the National Commission for Public Debate under conditions defined by regulation. III.-The guarantor is bound by an obligation of neutrality and impartiality and ensures in particular the quality, sincerity and intelligibility of the information disseminated to the public, the smooth running of the prior consultation and the possibility for the public to formulate questions, and present observations and proposals. It ensures the dissemination of all technical studies and expertise presented by the public during the participation procedure. L. 121-2 LAW no. 2018-148 of March 2, 2018 - art. 2 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf In the event of a risk of conflicts or disputes, the commission may be contacted for any project as defined in article
L. 122-1, by the parties concerned, when they are identified, of a common and reasoned request for conciliation with a view to achieving the resumption of dialogue between these parties and an agreement between them on the terms of participation of the public in the decision-making process. These parties include at least: - the project owner; - an association approved at national level, or two associations or a federation of associations approved under the article
L. 141-1 within the framework of the region or department territorially concerned. This referral does not have a suspensive nature for the current procedure. Section 2: Composition and operation of the National Commission for Public Debate
L. 121-3 ORDER no. 2015-948 of July 31, 2015 - art. 14 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The National Commission for Public Debate is made up of twenty-five members appointed for five years or for the duration of their mandate. In addition to its president and two vice-presidents, it includes: 1° A deputy and a senator appointed respectively by the President of the National Assembly and by the President of the Senate; 2° Six local elected officials appointed by decree on the proposal of the associations representing the elected officials concerned; 3° A member of the Council of State, elected by the general assembly of the Council of State; 4° A member of the Court of Cassation, elected by the general assembly of the Court of Cassation; 5° A member of the Court of Auditors, elected by the general assembly of the Court of Auditors; 6° A member of the body of members of administrative tribunals and administrative courts of appeal, appointed by decree on the proposal of the Superior Council of administrative tribunals and administrative courts of appeal; 7° Two representatives of environmental protection associations approved under article
L. 141-1 carrying out their activity throughout the national territory, appointed by order of the Prime Minister on the proposal of the Minister responsible for the environment; 8° Two representatives of consumers and users, respectively appointed by order of the Prime Minister on the proposal of the Minister responsible for the Economy and the Minister responsible for Transport; 9° Two qualified personalities, one of whom has exercised the functions of investigating commissioner, respectively appointed by order of the Prime Minister on the proposal of the Minister responsible for industry and the Minister responsible for equipment; 10° Two representatives of trade union organizations representing employees and two representatives of companies or consular chambers, including one representative of agricultural companies, appointed by order of the Prime Minister on the proposal of the most representative respective professional organizations. The two vice-presidents are a woman and a man. The members appointed on the proposal of the same authority in application of 2°, on the one hand, and all the members appointed in application of 7°, 8° and 9°, on the other hand, include an equal number of women and men. Each of the authorities called upon to appoint, propose or elect a member of the commission in application of 1°, 3° to 6° and 10° ensures that, after this appointment, proposal or election, the gap between the number of women and the number of men among all the members of the commission does not either not greater than one, or reduced when greater than two. L. 121-4 Ordinance No. 2016-1060 of August 3, 2016 - art. 2 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The commission can benefit from civil servants on secondment or the provision of civil servants in an active position. It can recruit contractual agents for the needs of its operation. To carry out its missions, it can appoint delegates in each region. The position of regional delegate gives rise to compensation.
L. 121-5 Ordinance No. 2016-1060 of August 3, 2016 - art. 2 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf In accordance with Law No. 2013-907 of October 11, 2013 relating to the transparency of public life, members of the National Commission and specific commissions interested in an operation in a personal capacity or because of their functions cannot participate in a debate or a prior consultation procedure relating to this operation.
L. 121-6 LAW n°2018-148 of March 2, 2018 - art. 2 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The credits necessary for the operation of the National Commission for Public Debate are included in the general State budget on the proposal of the Prime Minister. The president of the commission is the expenditure authorizer. He has authority over the services. The provisions of the law of August 10, 1922 relating to the organization of control of expenses incurred are not applicable to the expenses of the commission. To this end, a competition fund is created, within the meaning of II of article 17 of organic law no. 2001-692 of August 1, 2001 relating to finance laws, to contribute to expenses linked to public debate. This fund is intended to receive financial contributions. Expenses relating to the material organization of the public debate or prior consultation are the responsibility of the project owner or the public person responsible for developing the plan or program. When the project owner is not known at the time of the public debate, pre-financing of the latter is provided depending on the case by the State, one of its public establishments or one or more local authorities. As soon as it is known, the project owner reimburses the pre-financer. When the pre-financer is the State, reimbursement takes place by way of allocation of product, in application of III of article 17 of organic law no. 2001-692 of August 1, 2001 relating to finance laws. Compensation for the guarantors of the prior consultation designated by the National Commission for Public Debate and the cost of additional expertise are its responsibility. L. 141-1. This referral, accompanied by the reasons for the request, takes place within two months from the moment these projects are made public by the project owner. L. 122-4. L. 121-9 LAW n°2023-973 of October 23, 2023 - art. 5 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf When the National Commission for Public Debate is contacted, it determines the modalities of public participation in the decision-making process under the following conditions: 1° The commission assesses, for each project, plan or program whether the public debate must be organized according to its territorial impact, the socio-economic issues attached to it and its impacts on the environment or territorial planning. If the commission considers that a public debate is necessary, it organizes it and entrusts its animation to a specific commission that it constitutes. If the commission considers that a public debate is not necessary, it may decide to organize a prior consultation. It defines the terms, entrusts its organization to the project owner or the responsible public person and appoints a guarantor. The prior consultation thus carried out takes place under the conditions defined in section 4 of this chapter. When a project has been the subject of public debate during the development of a plan or program approved less than eight years ago and defining the framework within which the project could be authorized and implemented, the latter is exempt from public debate or prior consultation. The commission may, however, decide, if it considers it necessary, to organize such a debate or consultation and provide reasons for its decision; 2° When the National Commission for Public Debate is seized of a linear energy infrastructure project in application of article
L. 121-8, it organizes a prior consultation under the conditions set out in section 4 of this chapter; 3° The National Commission for Public Debate shall decide within two months on the follow-up to be made to the referrals provided for in I, II and IV of the article
L. 121-8. His decision is justified. It may decide not to resort to either a public debate or prior consultation. In the absence of an explicit decision at the end of this period, the commission is deemed to have given up on organizing the public debate or prior consultation.
L. 121-10 LAW n°2018-148 of March 2, 2018 - art. 2 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf When the Government wishes to organize a national public debate on the development of a reform project relating to a public policy having a significant effect on the environment or land use planning, it can refer the matter to the National Commission for Public Debate in view of the organization of public debate. This can also be requested by sixty deputies or sixty senators, or five hundred thousand adult nationals of the European Union residing in France, with a view to organizing such a debate. The committee indicates on its website that it has received a request for a debate and is organizing it under the conditions of this section. The minister(s) primarily concerned, the President of the National Assembly, the President of the Senate or the public person responsible for the policy mentioned in the first paragraph publish the lessons learned and the follow-up drawn from them.

Juricaf The National Commission for Public Debate establishes and publishes the schedule for the conduct of the public debate, the duration of which cannot exceed four months for projects and six months for plans and programs mentioned in IV of the article

The National Commission for Public Debate may ask the project owner or the responsible public person to complete the file that is planned to be submitted for public debate. The debate can only begin when the National Commission for Public Debate has considered the complete file. L. 121-12 LAW n°2018-148 of March 2, 2018 - art. 2 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The opening of the public inquiry provided for in article
L. 123-1 or public participation provided for in article
L. 123-19 relating to a project, plan or program falling under article
L. 121-8 can only be decided from the date from which a public debate or the prior consultation provided for in article
L. 121-8 can no longer be organized, either from the date of publication of the balance sheet or at the expiration of the period given to the president of the commission to proceed with this publication and at the latest within the period of eight years which follows these dates. Beyond this deadline, the commission can only decide to relaunch public participation if the factual or legal circumstances justifying the project, plan or program have undergone substantial modifications.
L. 121-13 LAW n°2018-148 of March 2, 2018 - art. 2 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf When a public debate has been organized on a plan, program or project, the project owner or the public person responsible for the development of the plan or program decides, within three months after publication of the assessment of the public debate, by an act which is published, of the principle and conditions of the continuation of the plan, program or project. It specifies, where applicable, the main modifications made to the plan, program or project submitted for public debate. It also indicates the measures it considers necessary to put in place to respond to the lessons it draws from the public debate. This act is transmitted to the National Commission for Public Debate. When the project owner or the public person responsible for the plan or program is a local authority or a public intermunicipal cooperation establishment, this act gives rise to a deliberation.
L. 121-14 LAW n°2018-148 of March 2, 2018 - art. 2 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf After a public debate or prior consultation decided by the National Commission for Public Debate, it appoints a guarantor responsible for ensuring good information and public participation until the opening of the public inquiry. The Commission determines the conditions under which the guarantor and the project owner or the responsible public person keep it informed. It ensures, if necessary, the publication of interim reports. The guarantor's final report is made public.
L. 121-15 Ordinance No. 2016-1060 of August 3, 2016 - art. 2 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf No irregularity with regard to the provisions of this chapter may be invoked when the act by which the National Commission for Public Debate has renounced organizing a public debate or a prior consultation or the act mentioned in article
L. 121-13 has become definitive. Section 4: Prior consultation Subsection 1: Scope of prior consultation

citizens 2° Projects subject to an environmental assessment pursuant to article
L. 122-1 and not falling within the scope of competence of the National Commission for Public Debate in application of I and II of the article

L. 122-4 and not falling within the scope of competence of the National Commission for Public Debate in application of Article IV
L. 121-8. The prior consultation makes it possible to discuss the opportunity, the objectives and the main characteristics of the project or the objectives and the main orientations of the plan or program, the socio-economic issues attached to it as well as their significant impacts on the environment and land use planning. This consultation makes it possible, where necessary, to discuss alternative solutions, including, for a project, its lack of implementation. It also concerns the methods of information and participation of the public after the prior consultation. However, projects and planning documents subject to compulsory consultation under article 2 cannot be the subject of prior consultation in application of 2° or 3°.
L. 103-2 of the town planning code, the projects having been the subject of consultation under article
L. 300-2 of the same code, organized in compliance with the rights mentioned in 1°, 3° and 4° of II of article
L. 120-1 of this code, as well as the following plans and programs subject to a specific procedure: -the technological risk prevention plan; -the flood risk management plan; -the water development and management master plan; -the action plan for the marine environment; -the overall plan of the public transport network of Greater Paris to which the public debate procedure provided for by Article 3 of Law No. 2010-597 of June 3, 2010 relating to Greater Paris is applicable. When the project is partly subject to compulsory consultation under 2°, 3° or 4° of the article
L. 103-2 of the town planning code and that it may also be partly subject to consultation under this section, the developer may make the choice, with the agreement of the competent authority mentioned to the article
L. 103-3 of the town planning code, to submit the entire project for consultation under this section according to the terms provided for in articles L. 121-16
L. 121-16 -1 of this code. This consultation takes the place of obligatory consultation under the article
L. 103-2 of the town planning code. Subsection 2: Conditions of prior consultation
L. 121-16 LAW n°2018-727 of August 10, 2018 - art. 57 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf Prior consultation involves the public in the development of a project, plan or program under the conditions defined by this section. The prior consultation lasts a minimum of fifteen days and a maximum of three months. Fifteen days before the start of the consultation, the public is informed of the terms and duration of the consultation by electronic means and by means of display at the place(s) concerned by the consultation as well as, depending on the importance and nature of the project, through local publication. The results of this consultation are made public. The project owner or the responsible public person indicates the measures he considers necessary to put in place to respond to the lessons he draws from the consultation. Expenses relating to the material organization of a prior consultation are the responsibility of the project owner or the public person responsible for the plan or program.

Book I: Common provisions - Title II: Information and participation of national citizens in the public debate of designate this guarantor from among those registered on the national list of guarantors mentioned in I of article e
L. 121-1 -1. II.-The guarantor may request a technical study or additional expertise from the National Commission for Public Debate, which bears the cost. The commission's decision is made known to the public on the website provided for the prior consultation. When receiving a request for the carrying out of a technical study or additional expertise, the guarantor reasons, where applicable, its decision not to forward this request for examination by the National Commission of public debate. Without prejudice to the provisions of Chapter I of Title I of Book III of the Code of Relations between the Public and the Administration, it rules, within the limits set by article
L. 311-5 of the said code, on the advisability of responding to requests for communication addressed either to the person having the status of project owner, or to the public authority competent to authorize the project or approve the plan or the program. He may address any request to the public person responsible for the plan or program or to the project owner to ensure good information and public participation. III.-The public may send its observations and proposals electronically or by post to the guarantor for publication on the website provided for prior consultation. In the event that consultation of a consultative body comprising representatives of the categories of people concerned by the decision in question is obligatory and when this takes place after the prior consultation, the summary of the observations and proposals of the public is sent to it beforehand. his opinion. IV.-The guarantor establishes within one month, at the end of the prior consultation, an assessment of it and summarizes the way in which it took place. This report includes a summary of the observations and proposals presented and, where applicable, mentions the developments in the project, plan or program which result from the prior consultation. The guarantor informs the project owner, the National Commission for Public Debate and the State representative of the progress and results of the prior consultation. The results of the prior consultation are made public by the guarantor.

The commission then determines the conditions under which the guarantor keeps it informed. The guarantor's final report is made public. Compensation for this guarantor is the responsibility of the project owner. Subsection 3: Initiation of prior consultation

L. 121-16. II. - In the absence of prior consultation decided in application of I, the competent authority to authorize a project mentioned in 2° of article
L. 121-15 -1 may require, by reasoned decision, the project owner to organize a prior consultation carried out in compliance with the terms defined in articles L. 121-16

L. 121-17 -1 LAW n°2020-1525 of December 7, 2020 - art. 40 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The right of initiative provided for in III of the article
L. 121-17 is open for: 1° The projects mentioned in 2° of the article
L. 121-15 -1, when the amount of forecast expenditure for such a project carried out under public project management is greater than a threshold set by decree of the Council of State and cannot be greater than 5 million euros , or when the total amount of public investment subsidies granted in the form of net financial aid to the owner of a private project is greater than this threshold; 2° The plans and programs mentioned in 3° of the article
L. 121-15 -1. This subsection is not applicable to projects, plans and programs for which the project owner or the responsible public person has organized prior consultation in accordance with the terms and conditions provided for in articles L. 121-16 L. 121-16
L. 121-18 LAW n°2020-1525 of December 7, 2020 - art. 43 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I.-For the projects mentioned in 1° of the article
L. 121-17 -1, a declaration of intent is published by the project owner before submitting the authorization request. No participation as defined in Chapter III can be undertaken in the absence of this publication. This declaration of intent is published on a website and includes the following elements: 1° The motivations and reasons for the project; 2° If applicable, the plan or program from which it arises; 3° The list of municipalities corresponding to the territory likely to be affected by the project; 4° An overview of the potential impacts on the environment; 5° A mention, where applicable, of the alternative solutions envisaged; 6° The modalities already envisaged, if necessary, for prior consultation with the public. II.-For the plans and programs mentioned in 2° of the article
L. 121-17 -1, the declaration of intent is constituted by the act prescribing their development as soon as it is published on a website. This act mentions, if necessary, the methods of prior consultation with the public envisaged if the declaration of intent has not been carried out until then. III.-Valent declaration of intent: 1° For the projects mentioned in 1° of the article
L. 121-17 -1, case-by-case decisions requiring an impact study mentioned in article

L. 121-17 -1 transmits its declaration of project intent to the competent administrative authority to authorize the project. The administrative authority competent to authorize the project informs the regions, departments and municipalities in which all or part of the territory mentioned in the declaration of intent is located. It can inform other local authorities as well as their groups. It may also inform associations or federations of associations approved under article
L. 141-1 within the framework of the region or department whose territory is included in whole or in part in that defined in the declaration of intent. Within one month from the transmission of the declaration of intent, the administrative authority competent to authorize the project may, if necessary, ask the project owner to provide additional information.
L. 121-19 LAW n°2020-1525 of December 7, 2020 - art. 43 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I. - The right of initiative mentioned in III of the article
L. 121-17 can be exercised with the representative of the State by: 1° A number of adult nationals of the European Union residing within the perimeter of the declaration of intent equal to 20% of the population listed in the municipalities of the same perimeter, or 10% of the population recorded in the department(s), in the region(s) where all or part of the territory mentioned in the declaration of intent is located; 2° A regional, departmental or municipal council or the deliberative body of a public intermunicipal cooperation establishment whose territory is included in whole or in part in that defined in the declaration of intent; 3° An association approved at the national level in application of article
L. 141-1, or two associations or a federation of associations approved under the article
L. 141-1 within the framework of the region or department whose territory is included in whole or in part in that defined in the declaration of intent. The right of initiative is exercised, at the latest, within two months following the publication of the declaration of intent for a project or, for plans and programs, of the act provided for in II of the article
L. 121-18. No prior consultation organized according to freely fixed terms may be implemented within this same period or before the decision of the representative of the State giving a favorable response to the request requesting the organization of a prior consultation. Within this period, only prior consultation respecting the terms set out in articles L. 121-16
L. 121-16 -1 can be initiated by the project owner or the responsible public person. II. - The State representative immediately informs the project owner or the public person responsible for developing the plan or program and, if different, the competent authority to authorize the project or approve the plan or program . It assesses the admissibility of the request, particularly with regard to the territory likely to be affected by the project, plan or program taking into account its main environmental impacts and its socio-economic benefits. The State representative decides on the advisability of organizing a prior consultation in accordance with the terms of articles L. 121-16
L. 121-16 -1 and, in this case, sets the duration and territorial scale of the participation which will be implemented with regard to the main environmental impacts and expected socio-economic benefits. Its decision is reasoned and made public within a maximum period of one month from receipt of the request. In the absence of an explicit decision within this period, the State representative is deemed to have rejected the request. p.34 Environmental Code http://legifrance.gouv.fr/affichTexte.do?cidTexte=JORFTEXT000042619877 https://legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/article_lc/LEGIARTI000042655088/2024-05-14 https:// www.courdecassation.fr/recherche-judilibre?search_api_fulltext=%22L%20121-19%22+%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&judilibre_juridiction=cc&op=Rechercher+sur+judilibre https://www.courdecassation.fr/recherche -judilibre?search_api_fulltext=%22L%20121-19%22+%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&judilibre_juridiction=ca&op=Rechercher+sur+judilibre https://legifrance.gouv.fr/search/cetat?tab_selection=cetat&searchField= ALL&query=%22article%20L121-19%22%20du%20%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&page=1&init=true&dateDecision= https://juricaf.org/recherche/article%20L.%20121-19%20% 22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22/facet_pays%3AFrance?tri=DESC Legislative part - Book I: Common provisions - Title II: Information and participation of citizens Subsection 5: Final provisions

Chapter II: Environmental assessment Section 1: Impact studies of works, structures and development projects s
L. 122-1 LAW n°2023-175 of March 10, 2023 - art. 5 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I.-For the application of this section, the following is understood as: 1° Project: the carrying out of construction work, installations or structures, or other interventions in the natural environment or landscape, including including those intended for the exploitation of soil resources; 2° Project owner: the author of an authorization request concerning a private project or the public authority which takes the initiative for a project; 3° Authorization: the decision of the competent authority or authorities which gives the project owner the right to carry out the project; 4° The competent authority: the authority(ies) competent to issue authorization for the project. II.-Projects which, by their nature, size or location, are likely to have significant impacts on the environment or human health are subject to an environmental assessment based on defined criteria and thresholds. by regulatory means and, for some of them, after a case-by-case examination. For the setting of these criteria and thresholds and for the determination of projects subject to a case-by-case examination, account shall be taken of the data mentioned in Annex III of Directive 2011/92/EU as amended of the European Parliament and of the Council of December 13, 2011 concerning the assessment of the impacts of certain public and private projects on the environment. When the authority responsible for the examination on a case-by-case basis decides to submit a project for environmental assessment, the The decision specifies the specific objectives pursued by carrying out the environmental assessment of the project. III.-Environmental assessment is a process consisting of the development, by the project owner, of an environmental impact assessment report, hereinafter referred to as an “impact study”, of the carrying out the consultations provided for in this section, as well as the examination, by the authority competent to authorize the project, of all the information presented in the impact study and received as part of the consultations carried out and the project owner. The environmental assessment makes it possible to describe and assess in an appropriate manner, depending on each particular case, the significant direct and indirect impacts of a project on the following factors: 1° The population and human health; 2° Biodiversity, paying particular attention to species and habitats protected under Directive 92/43/EEC of May 21, 1992 and Directive 2009/147/EC of November 30, 2009; 3° Land, soil, water, air and climate; 4° Material goods, cultural heritage and landscape; 5° The interaction between the factors mentioned in 1° to 4°. The impacts on the stated factors include the impacts likely to result from the vulnerability of the project to major accident risks and disasters relevant to the project concerned. When a project is made up of several works, installations, structures or other interventions in the natural environment or the landscape, it must be understood in s as a whole, including in the event of splitting in time and space and in the event of a multiplicity of project owners, so that its impacts on the environment are assessed in their entirety. p.36 Environmental Code http://legifrance.gouv.fr/affichTexte.do?cidTexte=JORFTEXT000047294244 https://legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/article_lc/LEGIARTI000047303065/2024-05-14 https:// www.courdecassation.fr/recherche-judilibre?search_api_fulltext=%22L%20122-1%22+%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&judilibre_juridiction=cc&op=Rechercher+sur+judilibre https://www.courdecassation.fr/recherche -judilibre?search_api_fulltext=%22L%20122-1%22+%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&judilibre_juridiction=ca&op=Rechercher+sur+judilibre https://legifrance.gouv.fr/search/cetat?tab_selection=cetat&searchField= ALL&query=%22article%20L122-1%22%20du%20%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&page=1&init=true&dateDecision= https://juricaf.org/recherche/article%20L.%20122-1%20% 22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22/facet_pays%3AFrance?tri=DESC Legislative part - Book I: Common provisions - Title II: Information and participation of citizens IV. - When a project is subject to a case-by-case examination, the authority in charge of the case-by-case examination is sent by the project owner a file presenting the project in order to determine whether it must be subject to environmental assessment. However, when the project consists of a modification or extension of activities, installations, works or works which fall under the authorizations provided for in articles L. 181-1 L. 512-7 L. 555- 1
L. 593-7, the project owner refers this file to the authority mentioned in article
L. 171-8. This authority determines whether this modification or extension must be subject to environmental assessment. V. - When a project is subject to environmental assessment, the file presenting the project including the impact study and the application for authorization filed is sent for opinion to the environmental authority as well as to the local authorities and their groups interested in the project. The opinions of local authorities and their groups and the opinion of the environmental authority, as soon as they are adopted, or information relating to the absence of observations issued within deadlines set by decree in the Council of State, as well as the written response of the project owner to the opinion of the environmental authority are made available to the public on the website of the competent authority or, failing that, on the website of the department prefecture. V bis. - The authority in charge of the case-by-case review and the environmental authority must not be in a position giving rise to a conflict of interest. For this purpose, an authority whose services or public establishments under its supervision are responsible for developing the project or ensuring its control cannot be designated as an authority in charge of the examination on a case-by-case basis or as an environmental authority. 'work. VI.-The project owners required to produce an impact study make it available to the public, as well as the written response to the opinion of the environmental authority, by electronic means at the latest at the time of opening of the public inquiry provided for in article
L. 123-2 or public participation by electronic means provided for in article
L. 123-19. service-public.fr > Installations classified for environmental protection (ICPE): Legislation applicable to environmental assessment > Installations, structures, works, activities (IOTA): Legislation applicable to environmental assessment
L. 122-1 -1 LAW n°2020-1525 of December 7, 2020 - art. 37 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I.-The competent authority to authorize a project subject to environmental assessment takes into consideration the impact study, the opinion of the authorities mentioned in Article V
L. 122-1 as well as the result of the public consultation and, where applicable, cross-border consultations. The decision of the competent authority is justified with regard to the significant impacts of the project on the environment. It specifies the requirements that the project owner must respect as well as the measures and characteristics of the project intended to avoid significant negative impacts, reduce those which cannot be avoided and compensate for those which cannot be avoided or reduced. It also specifies the methods for monitoring the impacts of the project on the environment or human health. The decision to refuse authorization sets out the reasons for the refusal, based in particular on the significant potential impacts of the project on the environment. II.-When a project subject to environmental assessment falls under a prior authorization regime which does not meet the conditions set out in I, the competent authority completes the authorization so that it complies. When a project subject to environmental assessment falls under a declaration regime, it is authorized by a decision of the authority competent to issue the declaration receipt, which contains the elements mentioned in I. When a project subject to environmental assessment does not fall under any particular authorization or declaration regime, it is authorized by the prefect by a decision which contains the elements mentioned in I. III.-The environmental impacts of a project whose implementation is subject to the issuance of several authorizations are assessed when the first authorization is issued. When the impacts of the project on the environment could not be completely identified or assessed before granting this authorization, the project owner updates the impact study by carrying out an evaluation of these impacts, within the scope of the operation for which authorization was requested and assessing p.37 Environmental Code https://entreprises.service-public.fr/vosdroits/F33414 https://entreprises.service-public.fr/ vosdroits/F36721 http://legifrance.gouv.fr/affichTexte.do?cidTexte=JORFTEXT000042619877 https://legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/article_lc/LEGI ARTI000042654900/2024-05-14 https://www.courdecassation.fr/recherche-judilibre?search_api_fulltext=%22L%20122-1-1%22+%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&judilibre_juridiction=cc&op=Rechercher+sur +judilibre https:/ /legifrance.gouv.fr/search/cetat?tab_selection=cetat&searchField=ALL&query=%22article%20L122-1-1%22%20du%20%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&page=1&init=true&dateDecision= https:// juricaf.org/recherche/article%20L.%20122-1-1%20%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22/facet_pays%3AFrance?tri=DESC Legislative part - Book I: Common provisions - Title II: Information and citizen participation their consequences on the overall scale of the project. L. 122-1 as well as their consideration; 3° The places where the impact study can be consulted.
L. 122-1 -2 LAW n°2018-148 of March 2, 2018 - art. 2 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf If the project owner requires it before submitting an authorization request, the competent authority issues an opinion on the scope and degree of precision of the information to be provided in the impact study. The competent authority consults the authorities mentioned in Article V

The clarifications provided by the competent authority do not prevent it from completing the authorization or approval application file and do not prejudge the decision that will be taken at the end of the investigation procedure.

Juricaf Expenditures incurred to carry out the controls, expertise or analyzes prescribed by the administrative authority to ensure compliance with the requirements, characteristics and measures established in application of the second paragraph of I of the article
L. 122-1 -1 are the responsibility of the project owner.
L. 122-3 -3 Ordinance No. 2016-1058 of August 3, 2016 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf When the inspection reveals a breach of the requirements, characteristics and measures established pursuant to the second paragraph of I of the article
L. 122-1 -1, the person who exercises it establishes a report which he transmits to the administrative authority. A copy of this report is delivered to the interested party, who can submit their observations within one month.

Section 2: Evaluation of certain plans and programs having a significant impact on the environment
L. 122-4 Ordinance No. 2020-7 of January 6, 2020 - art. 4 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I.-For the application of this section, the following is understood as: 1° “Plans and programs”: plans, diagrams, programs and other planning documents developed or adopted by the State, local authorities or their groups and public establishments depending on it, as well as their modification, as long as they are provided for by legislative or regulatory provisions, including those co-financed by the European Union; 2° “Environmental assessment”: a process consisting of the preparation of a report on the environmental impacts, the carrying out of consultations, the taking into account of this report and these consultations when making a decision by the authority which adopts or approves the plan or program, as well as publishing information about the decision, in accordance with Articles
L. 122-6 et seq. II.- Are subject to a systematic environmental assessment: 1° The plans and programs which are developed in the fields of agriculture, forestry, fishing, energy, industry, transport, waste management, water management, telecommunications, tourism or regional planning and which define the framework within which the projects mentioned in article
L. 122-1 may be authorized; 2° Plans and programs for which an assessment of Natura 2000 impacts is required pursuant to article
L. 414-4. III.- Are subject to a systematic environmental assessment or after examination on a case-by-case basis by the environmental authority: 1° The plans and programs mentioned in II which relate to small areas if they are likely to have significant impacts on the environment; 2° Plans and programs, other than those mentioned in II, which define the framework within which the implementation of projects may be authorized if these plans are likely to have significant impacts on the environment; 3° Modifications to the plans and programs mentioned in II and 1° and 2° if they are likely to have significant impacts on the environment. When the environmental authority decides to submit a plan or program for environmental assessment after examination on a case-by-case basis, the decision specifies the specific objectives pursued by carrying out the environmental assessment of the p lan or program. IV.-The significant environmental impacts of a plan or program or its modification are assessed taking into account the criteria mentioned in Annex II of Directive 2001/42/EC of Parliament p.40 Code of the environment http://legifrance.gouv.fr/affichTexte.do?cidTexte=JORFTEXT000039780349 https://legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/article_lc/LEGIARTI000041454254/2024-05-14 https://www.courdecassation. fr/recherche-judilibre?search_api_fulltext=%22L%20122-4%22+%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&judilibre_juridiction=cc&op=Rechercher+sur+judilibre https://www.courdecassation.fr/recherche-judilibre?search_api_fulltext =%22L%20122-4%22+%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&judilibre_juridiction=ca&op=Rechercher+sur+judilibre https://legifrance.gouv.fr/search/cetat?tab_selection=cetat&searchField=ALL&query=%22article %20L122-4%22%20du%20%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&page=1&init=true&dateDecision= https://juricaf.org/ research/article%20L.%20122-4%20%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22/facet_pays%3AFrance?tri=DESC Legislative part - Book I: Common provisions - Title II: Information and participation of European citizens and Council of June 27, 2001 relating to the assessment of the impacts of certain plans and programs on the environment. V.-Plans and programs established solely for national defense or civil protection purposes as well as financial or budgetary plans and programs are not subject to the obligation to carry out an environmental assessment. The authority responsible for developing the plan or program shall indicate to the environmental authority during the case-by-case examination, and to the competent authority when requesting an opinion on the impact report environmental, information of which it considers that its disclosure would be likely to harm the interests mentioned in I of the article THE
L. 124-4 and II of the article
L. 124-5. The following cannot appear in a file subject to public inquiry, nor be communicated, made available to the public or subject to public consultation or participation: - elements subject to rules for the protection of national defense secrecy; -the elements necessary to safeguard the interests of national defense; -elements whose disclosure would be likely to infringe manufacturing secrets; -elements likely to facilitate acts likely to harm public health, safety and sanitation. VI.-By way of derogation from the provisions of this code, the plans and programs mentioned in articles L. 104-1
L. 104-2 of the town planning code are subject to an environmental assessment under the conditions defined in chapter IV of the preliminary title of the town planning code.
L. 122-5 Ordinance No. 2016-1058 of August 3, 2016 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf A decree in the Council of State specifies in particular: 1° The list of plans and programs subject to environmental assessment in a systematic manner or to an examination on a case-by-case basis, in application of II and III of the article
L. 122-4 and the conditions for its annual updating; 2° The conditions under which, when a plan or program falls within the scope of II or III of Article
L. 122-4 but does not appear on the list established pursuant to 1°, the Minister responsible for the environment decides, for a period not exceeding one year, to submit it to systematic environmental assessment or examination in the case per case. The effects of this decision cease one year after its entry into force, or upon the entry into force of the next annual revision of the list mentioned in 1°, if it occurs earlier; 3° The terms and conditions of the exemptions provided for in Article V
L. 122-4; 4° The content of the report on environmental impacts mentioned in article
L. 122-6; 5° Cases in which modifications to plans and programs subject to environmental assessment may be subject to an environmental assessment after a case-by-case examination carried out by the environmental authority.
L. 122-6 LAW n°2018-148 of March 2, 2018 - art. 2 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The environmental assessment involves the establishment of a report which identifies, describes and evaluates the notable effects that the implementation of the plan or program may have on the environment as well as reasonable alternative solutions taking into account the objectives and of the geographical scope of the plan or program. This report presents the measures planned to avoid significant negative impacts that the application of the plan or program may have on the environment, the measures planned to reduce those which cannot be avoided and the measures planned to compensate for those which cannot be avoided or reduced. It sets out the other solutions considered and the reasons why, particularly from the point of view of environmental protection, the project was chosen. It defines the criteria, indicators and methods used to monitor the effects of the plan or program on the environment in order to identify, in particular, at an early stage, unforeseen negative impacts and consider, if necessary, appropriate measures. The environmental impact report regulations contains the information that may be reasonably required, taking into account the knowledge and evaluation methods existing on the date on which p.41 L. 122-7 Ordinance No. 2016-1058 of August 3, 2016 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The person responsible for developing a plan or program subject to environmental assessment pursuant to article
L. 122-4 transmits the draft plan or program accompanied by the report on environmental impacts to the environmental authority for opinion. The opinion, as soon as it is adopted, or the information relating to the absence of observations issued within the deadline, is posted online on its website. The environmental authority is consulted, as necessary, on the degree of precision of the information that the report on environmental impacts must contain.
L. 122-8 LAW n°2018-148 of March 2, 2018 - art. 2 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf Draft plans or programs whose implementation is likely to produce significant effects on the environment of another Member State of the European Union as well as reports on the environmental impacts of these projects are transmitted to the authorities of this State, at their request or at the initiative of the French authorities. The State concerned is invited to give its opinion within the deadline set by decree of the Council of State. In the absence of a response within this period, the notice is deemed issued. When a draft plan or program whose implementation is likely to produce notable effects on the national territory is sent for opinion to the French authorities by another State, it may be decided to consult the public on the project.
L. 122-9 LAW n°2018-148 of March 2, 2018 - art. 2 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I.-When the plan or program has been adopted, the authority which adopted it informs the public, the environmental authority and, where applicable, the authorities of the other Member States of the European Union consulted. It provides them with the following information: 1° The plan or program; 2° A declaration summarizing: - the manner in which the report drawn up pursuant to article
L. 122-6 and the consultations which were carried out; - the reasons which based the choices made by the plan or document, taking into account the various solutions envisaged; - measures intended to assess the environmental impacts of the implementation of the plan or program. II.-When a draft plan or program has not been submitted for environmental assessment after a case-by-case examination in application of Article III
L. 122-4, the public is informed of the reasoned decision of the environmental authority.
L. 122-10 Ordinance No. 2016-1058 of August 3, 2016 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The conditions of application of this section for each category of plans or programs are specified, as necessary, by decree in the Council of State. p.42 Environmental Code L. 122-11 Ordinance No. 2016-1058 of August 3, 2016 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf If a request filed before the administrative court against a decision to approve a plan or program referred to in article
L. 122-4 is based on the absence of an environmental assessment, the judge hearing a request for suspension of the contested decision grants it as soon as this absence is noted. Section 3: Common procedures and coordinates for environmental assessment Subsection 1: General provisions
L. 122-13 LAW n°2022-217 of February 21, 2022 - art. 259 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf A single environmental assessment procedure serving as both environmental assessment of the plan or program and of a project can be carried out at the initiative of the authority responsible for the plan or program and the project owner(s) concerned. , when the report on environmental impacts mentioned in Article
L. 122-6 contains the elements required under the project impact study mentioned in article
L. 122-1 and when the consultations required under section 1 and section 2 of this chapter are carried out. The environmental assessment procedure is said to be common when unique public consultation and participation procedures relate to both the plan or program and the project. When the project is subject to public inquiry, this procedure applies. The environmental assessment procedure is said to be coordinated when the developer of a project provided for by a plan or program, under which the public participation procedure and consultation of the authorities mentioned in Article V
L. 122-1 have been carried out under the conditions provided for in the first paragraph, is exempt from requesting a new opinion from the environmental authority and from conducting a new public participation procedure. A Council of State decree sets the terms and conditions for the implementation of common or coordinated environmental assessment procedures.
L. 122-14 LAW n°2022-217 of February 21, 2022 - art. 259 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf When the realization of a project subject to environmental assessment and subject to declaration of public utility or project declaration implies either the compatibility of an urban planning document also subject to environmental assessment in application of article
L. 122-4, i.e. the modification of a plan or program, the environmental assessment, when required, of the compatibility of this urban planning document or the modification of this plan or program and the impact study of the project can give rise to a common procedure. In this hypothesis, a common public participation procedure is organized. When the project or modification of the plan or program or the compatibility of the town planning document is subject to public inquiry, it is the latter procedure which applies. p.43 Environmental L. 122-15 LAW n°2022-217 of February 21, 2022 - art. 259 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf In Guyana and Mayotte, environmental assessments relating to projects may be the subject of a common procedure when these projects are located entirely or partly within the scope of an operation of national interest provided for in article
L. 102-12 of the town planning code. The files relating to these grouped environmental assessments may be established by an agent, who may be the competent public land and development establishment within the scope of the operation of national interest. These files indicate the information required from each project owner and specify the obligations incumbent on them under the environmental assessment. The decision of the competent authority sets the requirements provided for in I of Article
L. 122-1 -1 of this code, indicating, for each, the responsible project owners. service-public.fr > Installations classified for environmental protection (ICPE): Legislation applicable to environmental assessment > Installations, structures, works, activities (IOTA): Legislation applicable to environmental assessment Chapter III: Participation of public in decisions affecting the environment
L. 123-1 -A. LAW n°2023-973 of October 23, 2023 - art. 4 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf Chapter III applies to public participation: - for the projects mentioned in article
L. 122-1, after submission of the authorization request; - for the plans and programs mentioned in the article
L. 122-4, before the final phase of their adoption or approval; - other decisions that have an impact on the environment. This participation takes the form of: 1° A public inquiry in application of the articles
L. 123-1 et seq.; 2° Public participation in plans, programs and projects pursuant to article
L. 123-19 which is carried out electronically; 3° Public participation outside of a specific procedure in application of the articles
L. 123-19 -1 et seq.; 4° From the public consultation mentioned in the article
L. 181-10 -1, when applicable.

L. 123-1 Ordinance No. 2016-1060 of August 3, 2016 - art. 3 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The purpose of the public inquiry is to ensure information and participation of the public as well as taking into account the interests of third parties when developing decisions likely to affect the environment mentioned in article
L. 123-2. Observations and proposals received during the investigation period are taken into consideration by the project owner and the competent authority to make the decision.
L. 123-2 LAW n°2024-322 of April 9, 2024 - art. 50 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I.-The following are the subject of a public inquiry subject to the requirements of this chapter prior to their authorization, approval or adoption: 1° Projects for works, structures or developments carried out by public persons or private sector must include an environmental assessment pursuant to article
L. 122-1 with the exception of: - projects to which applies, under the first authorization mentioned in III of article
L. 122-1 -1, the public consultation provided for in article
L. 181-10 -1; - concerted development zone projects; - projects of a temporary or minor nature, the list of which is established by decree of the Council of State; - requests for building, development or demolition permits and prior declarations, provided for in Book IV of the town planning code, relating to work, construction or development projects giving rise to the realization of an environmental assessment after a case-by-case examination provided for in Article IV
L. 122-1 of this code. The application files for these planning authorizations are subject to a public participation procedure electronically according to the procedures provided for in article
L. 123-19 or the procedure provided for in article
L. 181-10 -1; - projects for artificial islands, installations, works and related installations on the continental shelf or in the exclusive economic zone; - projects which are located within the scope of an operation of national interest, within the meaning of the article
L. 102-12 of the town planning code, or a major town planning operation, within the meaning of article
L. 312-3 of the same code, and which meet the objectives of this operation, when public participation by electronic means is organized in application of article
L. 123-19 -11 of this code; 2° Plans, diagrams, programs and other planning documents subject to an environmental assessment pursuant to articles L. 122-4 L. 122-11 L. 104-1
L. 104-3 of the town planning code, for which a public inquiry is required in accordance with the legislation in force. However, when a change in plan or program is necessary to enable the completion of a project which is located within the scope of an operation of national interest or a major town planning operation and which meets the objectives of this operation, this public inquiry may be replaced by a public participation procedure by electronic means in application of article
L. 123-19 -11; 3° Projects for the creation of a national park, a marine natural park, projects for the charter of a national park or a regional natural park, projects for registration or classification of sites and projects for classification as a nature reserve and determination of their protection perimeter mentioned in Book III of this code; 4° Other planning documents and decisions relating to works, structures, developments, plans, diagrams and programs subject by the special provisions applicable to them to a public inquiry under the conditions of this chapter. p.45 Subsection 2: Procedure and conduct of the public inquiry eu
L. 123-3 LAW n°2023-175 of March 10, 2023 - art. 11 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The public inquiry is opened and organized by the competent authority to take the decision for which the inquiry is required. When the public inquiry concerns the project, plan, program or other planning document of a local authority, a public establishment of intermunicipal cooperation or one of the public establishments attached to them, it is opened by the president of the deliberative body of the community or establishment. However, when the investigation is prior to a declaration of public utility, the opening decision is taken by the State authority competent to declare the public utility. The authority competent to open and organize the investigation shall immediately inform the project owner of the operation subject to the public inquiry of the referral to the administrative court in whose jurisdiction the seat of this authority is located with a view to the appointment of an investigating commissioner or a commission of inquiry.
L. 123-4 LAW n°2023-175 of March 10, 2023 - art. 11 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf In each department, a commission chaired by the president of the administrative court or the advisor he delegates establishes a suitable list of investigating commissioners. This list is made public and is subject to at least one annual review. May be removed from this list any investigating commissioner having failed to fulfill the obligations defined in article
L. 123-15. The investigation is conducted, depending on the nature and importance of the operations, by an investigating commissioner or a commission of inquiry chosen by the president of the administrative court or the advisor delegated by him for this purpose from among the persons appearing on the lists of 'aptitude. The president of the administrative court or the advisor delegated by him also appoints one or more substitutes to the investigating commissioner or to the members of the commission of inquiry, who only intervene in the event of replacement, according to an order of appeal previously defined by the jurisdiction at the time of choosing the investigating commissioner or the commission of inquiry. Its choice is not limited to the lists of departments within the jurisdiction of the court. In the event that a prior consultation is held under the aegis of a guarantor in accordance with articles L. 121-16
L. 121-21, the president of the administrative court may appoint this guarantor as investigating commissioner if the latter is registered on one of the lists of suitable investigating commissioners. In the event that an investigating commissioner is unable to attend, the authority responsible for organizing the public inquiry shall immediately transfer the continuation of the investigation to a substitute commissioner, chosen by the administrative court under the conditions provided for in this paragraph. public survey. The public is informed of these decisions. p.46 Environmental Code http://legifrance.gouv.fr/affichTexte.do?cidTexte=JORFTEXT000047294244 https://legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/article_lc/LEGIARTI000047303134/2024-05-14 https:// www.courdecassation.fr/recherche-judilibre?sea rch_api_fulltext=%22L%20123-3%22+%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&judilibre_juridiction=cc&op=Rechercher+sur+judilibre https://www.courdecassation.fr/recherche-judilibre?search_api_fulltext=%22L%20123- 3%22+%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&judilibre_juridiction=ca&op=Rechercher+sur+judilibre https://legifrance.gouv.fr/search/cetat?tab_selection=cetat&searchField=ALL&query=%22article%20L123-3%22 %20du%20%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&page=1&init=true&dateDecision= https://juricaf.org/recherche/article%20L.%20123-3%20%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22/ facet_pays%3AFrance?tri=DESC http://legifrance.gouv.fr/affichTexte.do?cidTexte=JORFTEXT000047294244 https://legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/article_lc/LEGIARTI000047303128/2024-05-14 https:// www.courdecassation.fr/recherche-judilibre?search_api_fulltext=%22L%20123-4%22+%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&judilibre_juridiction=cc&op=Rechercher+sur+judilibre https://www.courdecassation.fr/recherche -judilibre?search_api_fulltext=%22L%20123-4%22+%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&judilibre_juridiction=ca&op=Rechercher+sur+judilibre https://legifrance.gouv.fr/search/cetat?tab_selection=cetat&searchField= ALL&query=%22article%20L123-4%22%20du%20%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&page=1&init=true&dateDecision= https://juricaf.org/recherche/article%20L.%20123-4%20% 22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22/facet_pays%3AFrance?tri=DESC Legislative part - Book I: Common provisions - Title II: Information and participation of citizens ns
L. 123-5 LAW no. 2019-1461 of December 27, 2019 - art. 81 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf Persons interested in the project in a personal capacity, because of their elective functions exercised in the territory concerned by the public inquiry, or because of their functions, in particular at the within the community, organization or service which is responsible for project management, project management or control of the operation subject to investigation. The provisions of the first paragraph may be extended, under the conditions set by decree of the Council of State, to persons who have held these functions.
L. 123-6 LAW n°2023-175 of March 10, 2023 - art. 11 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I. - When the implementation of a project, plan or program is subject to the organization of several public consultations, at least one of which pursuant to article
L. 123-2, a single public inquiry governed by this section may be carried out as long as the authorities competent to take the decision designate by mutual agreement the one who will be responsible for opening and organizing this inquiry. In the absence of this agreement, and at the request of the project owner or the responsible public person, the representative of the State, as long as he is competent to take one of the authorization or approval decisions envisaged, may open and organize the single investigation. Under the same conditions, a single survey may also be carried out when public consultations on several projects, plans or programs can be organized simultaneously and the organization of such a survey contributes to improving information and participation of the audience. The duration of the public inquiry cannot be less than the minimum duration of the longest provided for by one of the legislations concerned. The file submitted to a single public inquiry includes the documents or elements required for each of the public consultations initially required and a non-technical presentation note of the project(s), plans or programs. This unique investigation is the subject of a single report from the investigating commissioner or the commission of inquiry as well as reasoned conclusions for each of the public consultations initially required. II. - In the event of contestation of a decision taken at the end of a public inquiry organized under the conditions of this article, the regularity of the file is assessed with regard to the specific rules applicable to the contested decision.
L. 123-7 LAW n°2023-973 of October 23, 2023 - art. 4 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf When a project of works, structures or developments is likely to have significant impacts on the environment of another State, member of the European Community or party to the convention of February 25, 1991 on the assessment of the impact on the environment in a transboundary context signed in Espoo, the information enabling public information and participation is transmitted to the authorities of this State, at their request or at the initiative of the authorities French. The authorities of the State concerned are invited to participate in the public inquiry provided for in article
L. 123-1, to the public participation procedure by electronic means provided for in article
L. 123-19 or the public consultation provided for in article L. 181-10
L. 123-8 Ordinance No. 2016-1060 of August 3, 2016 - art. 3 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf When a project of works, structures or developments likely to have significant impacts on the environment in France is sent for opinion to the French authorities by a State, the public is consulted by a public inquiry carried out in accordance with the this chapter. The public inquiry is opened and organized by order of the prefect of the department concerned. After the investigation is closed, the prefect transmits his opinion to the authorities of the State in whose territory the project is located. This opinion is accompanied by the report and the opinion of the investigating commissioner or the commission of inquiry. The decision taken by the competent authority of the State in whose territory the project is located is made available to the public at the prefecture of the department(s) in which the investigation was organized. p.47 Environmental Code http://legifrance.gouv.fr/affichTexte.do?cidTexte=JORFTEXT000039681877 https://legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/article_lc/LEGIARTI000041411850/2024-05-14 https:// www.courdecassation.fr/recherche-judilibre?search_api_fulltext=%22L%20123-5%22+%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&judilibre_juridiction=cc&op=Rechercher+sur+judilibre https://www.courdecassation.fr/recherche -judilibre?search_api_fulltext=%22L%20123-5%22+%22Code%20de%20l%27environment%22& judilibre_juridiction=ca&op=Rechercher+sur+judilibre https://legifrance.gouv.fr/search/cetat?tab_selection=cetat&searchField=ALL&query=%22article%20L123-5%22%20du%20%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement% 22&page=1&init=true&dateDecision= https://juricaf.org/recherche/article%20L.%20123-5%20%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22/facet_pays%3AFrance?tri=DESC http://legifrance. gouv.fr/affichTexte.do?cidTexte=JORFTEXT000047294244 https://legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/article_lc/LEGIARTI000047303124/2024-05-14 https://www.courdecassation.fr/recherche-judilibre?search_api_fulltext=%22L %20123-6%22+%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&judilibre_juridiction=cc&op=Rechercher+sur+judilibre https://www.courdecassation.fr/recherche-judilibre?search_api_fulltext=%22L%20123-6%22+ %22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&judilibre_juridiction=ca&op=Rechercher+sur+judilibre https://legifrance.gouv.fr/search/cetat?tab_selection=cetat&searchField=ALL&query=%22article%20L123-6%22%20du%20 %22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&page=1&init=true&dateDecision= https://juricaf.org/recherche/article%20L.%20123-6%20%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22/facet_pays%3AFrance?
