L. 123-9 Ordinance No. 2016-1060 of August 3, 2016 - art. 3 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The duration of the public inquiry is set by the competent authority responsible for opening and organizing it. It cannot be less than thirty days for projects, plans and programs subject to an environmental assessment. The duration of the investigation may be reduced to fifteen days for a project, plan or program not subject to an environmental assessment. By reasoned decision, the investigating commissioner or the president of the commission of inquiry may extend the investigation for a maximum period of fifteen days, in particular when he decides to organize an information and discussion meeting with the public during this period of extension of the investigation. This decision is brought to the attention of the public, at the latest on the date initially planned for the end of the investigation, under the conditions provided for in I of article L. 123-10
L. 123-10 Ordinance No. 2017-80 of January 26, 2017 - art. 2 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I.-At least fifteen days before the opening of the investigation and during it, the authority competent to open and organize the investigation informs the public. Public information is provided electronically and by display at the location(s) concerned by the investigation, as well as, depending on the importance and nature of the project, plan or program, by local publication. This opinion specifies: -the purpose of the investigation; -the decision(s) that may be adopted at the end of the investigation and the authorities competent to rule; -the name and qualifications of the investigating commissioner or members of the commission of inquiry; -the date of opening of the investigation, its duration and methods; -the address of the website(s) on which the investigation file can be consulted; -the place(s) as well as the times where the investigation file can be consulted on paper and the investigation register accessible to the public; -the access point(s) and times where the public inquiry file can be consulted on a computer station; -the address(es) to which the public can send their observations and proposals during the investigation period. If there is a dematerialized register, this notice specifies the address of the website at which it is accessible. The notice also indicates the existence of a report on environmental impacts, an impact study or, failing that, a file including environmental information relating to the subject of the investigation, and the address of the website as well as the place(s) where these documents can be consulted if they differ from the address and places where the file can be consulted. It states, when they have been issued, the existence of the opinion of the environmental authority mentioned in V of article L. 122-1
L. 122-7 of this code or article
L. 104-6 of the town planning code, and the opinions of local authorities and their groups mentioned in Article V
L. 122-1 of this code, as well as the place or places where they can be consulted and the address of the websites where they can be consulted if it differs from that mentioned above. II.-The person responsible for the project assumes the costs relating to these various publicity measures for the public inquiry.

L. 123-12 Ordinance No. 2016-1060 of August 3, 2016 - art. 3 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The public inquiry file is posted online for the duration of the investigation. It remains available for consultation, for the same period, on paper in one or more locations determined upon the opening of the public inquiry. Free access to the file is also guaranteed by one or more computer stations in a place open to the public. If the project, plan or program has been the subject of a public debate procedure organized under the conditions defined in articles L. 121-8
L. 121-15, or a prior consultation organized under the conditions defined in articles L. 121-16
L. 121-16 -1, or any other procedure provided for by the texts in force allowing the public to participate effectively in the decision-making process, the file includes the results of this procedure as well as the summary of the observations and proposals made by the public . When no prior consultation has taken place, the file mentions this.
L. 123-13 LAW n°2018-148 of March 2, 2018 - art. 2 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I. - The investigating commissioner or the commission of inquiry conducts the investigation in such a way as to allow the public to have complete information on the project, plan or program, and to participate effectively in the decision-making process. He or she allows the public to send their observations and proposals during the duration of the investigation by electronic mail systematically as well as by any other method specified in the order opening the investigation. Observations and proposals transmitted electronically are accessible on a website designated by regulation. II. - During the investigation, the investigating commissioner or the president of the commission of inquiry receives the client of the operation subject to the public inquiry at the request of the latter. He may also: - receive any information and, if he considers that documents are useful for the proper information of the public, ask the project owner to communicate these documents to the public; - visit the premises concerned, with the exception of residential premises, after having previously informed the owners and occupants; - hear all the people concerned by the project, plan or program who request it and summon all the people whose hearing it deems useful; - organize, under his chairmanship, any information and exchange meeting with the public in the presence of the project owner. At the request of the investigating commissioner or the president of the commission of inquiry and when the specificities of the investigation require it, the president of the administrative court or the advisor he delegates may appoint an expert responsible for assisting the investigating commissioner. or the commission of inquiry. The cost of this additional expertise is the responsibility of the project manager.
L. 123-14 LAW n°2018-727 of August 10, 2018 - art. 62 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I.-During the public inquiry, if the person responsible for the project, plan or program referred to in I of article

This suspension option can only be used once. L. 122-7 of this code and article
L. 104-6 of the town planning code as well as, where applicable, to local authorities and their groups consulted in application of article V
L. 122-1. At the end of this period and after the public has been informed of the modifications made under the conditions defined in article
L. 123-10 of this code, the investigation is extended for a period of at least thirty days. p.49 Environmental Code http://legifrance.gouv.fr/affichTexte.do?cidTexte=JORFTEXT000032966914 referred to in I of article e
L. 123-2 may, if it considers it desirable to make changes to it which modify its general structure, ask the organizing authority to open a complementary investigation into the advantages and disadvantages of these modifications for the project and for the environment. L. 123-15 LAW n°2023-175 of March 10, 2023 - art. 7 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The investigating commissioner or the commission of inquiry submits its report and its reasoned conclusions within thirty days from the end of the investigation. If this deadline cannot be respected, additional time may be granted at the request of the investigating commissioner or the commission of inquiry by the authority competent to organize the investigation, after consulting the project manager. For renewable energy production installation projects, within the meaning of article
L. 211-2 of the energy code, and within the strict limits of the acceleration zones for the establishment of land-based renewable energy production facilities provided for in article
L. 141-5 -3 of the same code, the investigating commissioner or the commission of inquiry renders its report and its reasoned conclusions within fifteen days from the end of the investigation. If this deadline cannot be respected, the additional period provided for in the first paragraph of this article cannot exceed fifteen days. The report must state the observations and proposals that were produced during the investigation as well as any responses from the project owner. The report and the reasoned conclusions are made public electronically on the public inquiry website and where they can be consulted in paper form. If, at the expiration of the deadlines provided for in the first and second paragraphs, the investigating commissioner or the commission of inquiry has not submitted its report and its reasoned conclusions, nor provided a reason for exceeding the deadline, the authority competent to organize the investigation may, with the agreement of the project owner and after formal notice from the investigating commissioner or the commission of inquiry which remains unsuccessful, ask the president of the administrative court or the advisor he delegates to remove the investigating commissioner or the commission of inquiry and replace it with a new investigating commissioner or a new commission of inquiry; the latter must, based on the results of the investigation, submit the report and the reasoned conclusions within a maximum of thirty days from his appointment. The new investigating commissioner or the new commission of inquiry may use the prerogatives provided for in article
L. 123-13. The authority competent to take the decision may organize, in the presence of the project owner, a public meeting in order to respond to any reservations, recommendations or unfavorable conclusions of the investigating commissioner or the commission of inquiry. It is organized within two months after the conclusion of the investigation. The investigating commissioner or the commission of inquiry is informed of the holding of such a meeting.

L. 123-19 LAW n°2023-973 of October 23, 2023 - art. 4 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I. - Public participation is carried out electronically. It is applicable: 1° To projects which are the subject of an environmental assessment and which are exempt from public inquiry in application of 1° of I of the article
L. 123-2, if they are not subject to public consultation provided for in article
L. 181-10 -1; 2° To plans and programs which are the subject of an environmental assessment pursuant to articles L. 122-4 L. 122-11 L. 104-1
L. 104-3 of the town planning code and for which a public inquiry is not required in application of the specific provisions which govern them. As an exception to the previous paragraph, water development and management master plans, flood risk management plans and action plans for the marine environment are subject to specific public participation provisions. Public participation by electronic means is open and organized by the competent authority to authorize these projects or approve these plans and programs. II. - The file submitted to this procedure includes the same documents as those provided for in article
L. 123-12. It is made available to the public electronically and, upon request presented under conditions provided for by decree, made available for consultation on paper in the prefectures and sub-prefectures as well as in the France Services spaces and in the town hall of the municipality. implementation of the project with regard to the decisions of State authorities, including independent administrative authorities, and public establishments of the State, or at the headquarters of the authority as well as in the France Services spaces and in the town hall of the municipality where the project is located with regard to the decisions of other authorities. When the volume or characteristics of the draft decision or application file do not allow it to be made available electronically, the presentation note specifies the purpose of the participation procedure, the places and times where the entire project or the application file can be consulted. Within the France Services spaces, an agent may be responsible for supporting people in difficulty with IT in their procedures relating to public participation electronically. p.51 Environmental Code and participation of citizens The public is informed by a notice posted online as well as by a display in town hall or at the places concerned and, depending on the importance and nature of the project, by local publication fifteen days before the opening of electronic public participation for plans, programs and projects. This notice mentions: 1° The draft plan or program or the request for authorization of the project; 2° The contact details of the authorities competent to make the decision, those from which relevant information can be obtained, those to whom observations or questions can be addressed as well as details of the conditions under which they can be issued; 3° The decision(s) that may be adopted at the end of participation and the authorities competent to rule; 4° An indication of the date on which and place where the relevant information will be made available to the public and the conditions of this making available; 5° The address of the website on which the file can be consulted; 6° The fact that the plan or program or project is subject to environmental assessment and that, where applicable, it is likely to have significant impacts on the environment of another Member State under the conditions provided for in artic THE
L. 123-7 and the place where this report or this impact study can be consulted; 7° When it has been issued, the opinion of the environmental authority mentioned in article L. 122-7
L. 104-6 of the town planning code as well as the place(s) where it can be consulted. Expenses relating to the material organization of this participation are the responsibility of the project owner or the public person responsible for the plan or program. Comments and proposals from the public, submitted electronically, must reach the administrative authority concerned within a period which cannot be less than thirty days from the start date of electronic public participation. III. - The provisions of the last three paragraphs of II of the article are applicable to public participation carried out under this article.
L. 123-19 -1, as well as the provisions of articles L. 123-19
L. 123-19 -5. Section 3: Public participation outside specific procedures
L. 123-19 -1 Ordinance No. 2016-1060 of August 3, 2016 - art. 2 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I.-This article defines the conditions and limits in which the principle of public participation, provided for in Article 7 of the Environmental Charter, is applicable to decisions, other than individual decisions, of public authorities having an impact on the environment when they are not subject, by the legislative provisions applicable to them, to a specific procedure organizing public participation in their development. The provisions of this article do not apply to decisions which modify, extend, withdraw or repeal the decisions mentioned in the preceding paragraph subject to a specific procedure organizing public participation in their development. Decisions which have an indirect or insignificant effect on the environment are not considered to have an impact on the environment. II.-Subject to the provisions of article
L. 123-19 -6, the draft of a decision mentioned in I, accompanied by a presentation note specifying in particular the context and objectives of this project, is made available to the public electronically and, upon request submitted under conditions provided for by decree, placed for consultation on paper in the prefectures and sub-prefectures with regard to the decisions of State authorities, including independent administrative authorities, and public establishments of the State, or at the seat of the authority with regard to the decisions of other authorities. When the volume or characteristics of the draft decision do not allow it to be made available electronically, the presentation note specifies the places and times where the entire draft can be consulted. p.52 Environmental Code https://legifrance.gouv.fr/affichCodeArticle.do?cidTexte=LEGITEXT000006074075&idArticle=LEGIARTI000031210153&dateTexte=&categorieLien=cid https://legifrance.gouv.fr/affichCodeArticle.do?cidTexte=LEGITEXT0000060740 75&idArticle=LEGIARTI000031210153&dateText =&categorieLien=cid http://legifrance.gouv.fr/affichTexte.do?cidTexte=JORFTEXT000032966914 https://legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/article_lc/LEGIARTI000032975930/2024-05-14 https://www.courdecassation. fr/recherche-judilibre?search_api_fulltext=%22L%20123-19-1%22+%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&judilibre_juridiction=cc&op=Rechercher+sur+judilibre https://www.courdecassation.fr/recherche-judilibre ?search_api_fulltext=%22L%20123-19-1%22+%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&judilibre_juridiction=ca&op=Rechercher+sur+judilibre https://legifrance.gouv.fr/search/cetat?tab_selection=cetat&searchField= ALL&query=%22article%20L123-19-1%22%20du%20%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&page=1&init=true&dateDecision= https://juricaf.org/recherche/article%20L.%20123-19- 1%20%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22/facet_pays%3AFrance?tri=DESC Legislative part - Book I: Common provisions - Title II: Information and participation of citizens For decisions of national scope by the State, including including independent administrative authorities, and public establishments of the State, the indicative list of scheduled consultations is published every three months electronically. At the latest on the date of making available p revised in the first paragraph of this II, the public is informed, electronically, of the consultation methods adopted. Comments and proposals from the public, submitted electronically or by post, must reach the administrative authority concerned within a period which cannot be less than twenty-one days from the date of making them available provided for in the same first paragraph. The draft decision cannot be definitively adopted before the expiration of a period allowing observations and proposals submitted by the public to be taken into consideration and a summary of these observations and proposals to be drawn up. Except in the absence of observations and proposals, this deadline cannot be less than four days from the date of closure of the consultation. In the event that consultation of a consultative body comprising representatives of the categories of people concerned by the decision in question is obligatory and when this takes place after the public consultation, the summary of the public's observations and proposals is sent to it in advance. in his opinion. At the latest on the date of publication of the decision and for a minimum period of three months, the administrative authority which took the decision makes public, by electronic means, the summary of the observations and proposals of the public with the indication of those taken into account, the observations and proposals filed electronically as well as, in a separate document, the reasons for the decision. III.-By way of derogation from II, public participation in the development of decisions by the authorities of municipalities with less than 10,000 inhabitants can be organized under the following conditions. The purpose of the participation procedure as well as the places and times where the draft decision accompanied by the presentation note can be consulted and where observations and proposals can be filed in a register are made known to the public by means of display in town hall. This posting specifies the deadline within which these observations and proposals must be submitted, which cannot be less than twenty-one days from the start of the posting. In the event that the municipality has a website, the information mentioned in the previous paragraph as well as the note presentation and, unless its volume or characteristics do not allow it, the draft decision are also made available to the public electronically for the same period. The draft decision cannot be definitively adopted before the expiration of a period allowing observations and proposals from the public to be taken into consideration. Unless there are no observations and proposals, this deadline cannot be less than four days from the closing date of the consultation. At the latest on the date of publication of the decision and for a minimum period of one month, the mayor makes public, by posting, a summary of the public's observations and proposals or indicates, by the same means, the locations and times when the register for collecting observations and proposals is made available to the public for the same period. The provisions of this III apply to the decisions of the authorities of the community of Saint-Martin and those of the territorial community of Saint-Pierre and Miquelon, as well as to the decisions of the authorities of groupings of local authorities whose total population is less than 30,000 inhabitants. In this case, the display is made at the group's headquarters. IV.-By way of derogation from II and III, public participation in the development of decisions by the authorities of municipalities with less than 2,000 inhabitants may be organized within the framework of a public meeting. The purpose of the participation procedure as well as the place, date and time of the meeting are made known to the public by posting in the town hall, within a period which cannot be less than eight days before the date planned for the meeting. holding the meeting. The display specifies the places and times where the draft decision can be consulted. The draft decision cannot be definitively adopted before the expiration of a period allowing observations and proposals from the public to be taken into consideration, which cannot be less than four days from the date of the public meeting. In the event of no comments, this period cannot be less than four days from the date of closure of the consultation. p.53 Environmental Code Legislative part - Book I: Common provisions - Title II: Information and participation of citizens ns
L. 123-19 -2 Ordinance No. 2016-1060 of August 3, 2016 - art. 2 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I.-Subject to the provisions of article
L. 123-19 -6, this article defines the conditions and limits under which the principle of public participation provided for in Article 7 of the Environmental Charter is applicable to individual decisions of public authorities having an impact on the environment which do not belong to a category of decisions for which specific legislative provisions have provided for the cases and conditions in which they must, where appropriate based on thresholds and criteria, be subject to public participation. Decisions which modify, extend, withdraw or repeal a decision belonging to such a category are also not subject to the provisions of this article. Decisions which have an indirect or insignificant effect on the environment are not considered to have an impact on the environment. The provisions of this article do not apply: 1° To decisions for which the public authorities have no power of appreciation; 2° To decisions having the character of a formal notice or a sanction. II.-The draft of a decision mentioned in I or, when the decision is taken upon request, the application file is made available to the public electronically. When the volume or characteristics of the draft decision or application file do not allow its made available electronically, the public is informed, electronically, of the subject of the participation procedure and the places and times where the entire project or application file can be consulted. At the latest on the date of availability or information provided for in the preceding paragraph, the public is informed, electronically, of the terms of the participation procedure adopted. Comments and proposals from the public, submitted electronically, must reach the public authority concerned within a period which cannot be less than fifteen days from the date of making them available. The draft decision cannot be definitively adopted before the expiration of a period allowing observations and proposals submitted by the public to be taken into consideration. Unless there are no observations and proposals, this deadline cannot be less than three days from the closing date of the consultation. III.-By way of derogation from II, public participation in the development of decisions by the authorities of municipalities with less than 10,000 inhabitants can be organized under the following conditions. The subject of the participation procedure as well as the places and times where the draft decision or, where the decision is taken upon request, the application file can be consulted and where observations and Proposals can be placed in a register and made known to the public by posting in the town hall. This posting specifies the deadline within which these observations and proposals must be submitted, which cannot be less than fifteen days from the start of the posting. In the event that the municipality has a website, the information mentioned in the previous paragraph as well as, unless its volume or characteristics do not allow it, the draft decision or the application file are also made available. available to the public electronically for the same period. The draft decision cannot be definitively adopted before the expiration of a period allowing observations and proposals from the public to be taken into consideration. Unless there are no observations and proposals, this deadline cannot be less than three days from the closing date of the consultation. The provisions of this III apply to decisions by the authorities of groups of local authorities whose total population is less than 30,000 inhabitants. In this case, the display is made at the group's headquarters. The provisions of this III also apply to decisions taken by the authorities, respectively, of the community of Saint-Martin and the territorial community of Saint-Pierre-et-Miquelo not.

L. 123-19 -11 LAW n°2024-322 of April 9, 2024 - art. 50 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf When a project located within the scope of an operation of national interest, within the meaning of article
L. 102-12 of the town planning code, or a major town planning operation, within the meaning of article
L. 312-3 of the same code, and meeting the objectives of this operation or any development of plan or program made necessary to enable its implementation must be the subject of a public inquiry subject to the requirements of Chapter III of Title II of Book I of this code or the public consultation procedure provided for in article
L. 181-10 -1, public participation may be carried out by electronic means under the conditions defined in article
L. 123-19. When the realization of a project or the evolution of a plan or program mentioned in the first paragraph of this article is subject to the organization of several participations by electronic means, one participation may be carried out by electronic means unique ; the authorities competent to make the decision agree on which one will be responsible for opening and organizing this participation. In the absence of agreement, at the request of the project owner or the responsible public person, the representative of the State, when competent to take one of the authorization or approval decisions envisaged, may open and organize participation electronically. Under the same conditions, a single electronic participation may also be carried out when electronic participation concerning several projects or developments of plans or programs can be organized simultaneously and the organization of such electronic participation contributes to improving public information and participation. To enable the completion of a project mentioned in the first paragraph of this article, when recourse is had to a declaration involving the compatibility of a planning or town planning document or to an integrated procedure provided for in article
L. 300-6 -1 of the town planning code, public participation by electronic means must relate both to the general interest of the operation and to the compatibility which is the consequence. p.56 L. 123-19 -12 LAW n°2024-322 of April 9, 2024 - art. 50 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf A decree of the Council of State specifies the conditions of application of this chapter. Chapter III bis: Local consultation on projects likely to have an impact on the environment Section 1: General provisions
L. 123-20 Ordinance No. 2016-488 of April 21, 2016 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The State may consult the voters of a specific territorial area in order to obtain their opinion on an infrastructure or equipment project likely to have an impact on the environment, the implementation of which is subject to the issuance of a authorization falling within its competence, including after a declaration of public utility.
L. 123-21 Ordinance No. 2016-488 of April 21, 2016 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The area of ​​the consultation corresponds to that of the territory covered by the public inquiry to which this project was the subject or, when several public inquiries have been carried out under separate legislations, to that of the entire territory covered through these surveys. The territory covered by the investigation is that of the municipalities designated as places of investigation by the order opening the investigation as well as, when the capital of an administrative district of the State has also been designated as place of investigation, the territory of the municipalities included in this constituency. In other cases, the consultation area is that of the territory of the municipalities whose environment is likely to be affected by the project. The consultation area is indicated by the decree provided for in article L. 123-23
L. 123-22 Ordinance No. 2016-488 of April 21, 2016 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf Only voters of French nationality registered, under the conditions provided for in Chapter II of Title I of Book I of the Electoral Code, on the electoral lists of the municipalities in which the consultation is organized and nationals of a Member State of the European Union registered, under the conditions provided for in articles LO 227-1 to LO 227-5 of the same code, on the complementary electoral lists of these same municipalities established for municipal elections. p.57 Environmental Code Information and citizen participation ns
L. 123-23 Ordinance No. 2016-488 of April 21, 2016 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The consultation is decided by a decree which indicates the object, the date as well as the scope, which defines the question asked and which summons the voters. It is published at the latest two months before the date of the consultation. The consultation cannot take place after the first day of the third month preceding that during which the elections and ballots listed in the fifth to tenth paragraphs of article LO 1112-6 of the general code of local authorities are held. Section 2: Organization of the consultation
L. 123-24 Ordinance No. 2016-488 of April 21, 2016 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The decree provided for by article
L. 123-23 is notified within two weeks of its publication by the State representative in the department to the mayors of the municipalities concerned. In accordance with the obligation imposed on them by 3° of article
L. 2122-27 of the general code of local authorities, mayors ensure the provision of information to voters and the organization of consultation operations under the conditions provided for in this chapter. The State is responsible for all expenses relating to the consultation.
L. 123-25 ​​Ordinance No. 2016-488 of April 21, 2016 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf From the date of publication of the decree provided for in article
L. 123-23, the prohibitions and restrictions provided for by articles L. 47 to L. 50-1 L. 52-1
L. 52-2 of the electoral code are applicable to any propaganda action relating to the project which is the subject of the consultation or to it. The provisions of Law No. 77-808 of July 19, 1977 relating to the publication and dissemination of certain opinion polls also apply.
L. 123-26 Ordinance No. 2016-488 of April 21, 2016 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf An information file on the project which is the subject of the consultation is drawn up by the National Commission for Public Debate. This file includes a summary document presenting in a clear and objective manner the project, its reasons, its characteristics, the progress of the procedures, its impacts on the environment and the other effects expected from it. It mentions the main documents likely to enlighten voters and includes links to the websites where these documents can be consulted. The file is posted online on the website of the National Commission for Public Debate at least fifteen days before the date set for the consultation. The mayors provide voters with an internet access point which allows them to read them. The decree provided for in article
L. 123-23 may provide for additional arrangements for making this file available to voters when they prove necessary.
L. 123-27 Ordinance No. 2016-488 of April 21, 2016 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf An information letter relating to the organization of the consultation accompanied by two ballot papers is sent by the State to each voter no later than the third Thursday preceding the L. 123-28 Ordinance No. 2016-488 of April 21, 2016 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf Voters indicate their opinion on the question posed to them by “YES” or “NO”.
L. 123-29 Ordinance No. 2016-488 of April 21, 2016 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf Voting operations for the consultation are governed by the provisions of Chapter VI of Title I of Book I of the electoral code, with the exception of articles
L. 52-19, L. 56, L. 57, L. 58, L. 67, the second paragraph of article L. 68 and article
L. 85-1, with the following adaptations: 1° For the application of the third paragraph of Article L. 65, the words: - “the names given” are replaced by the words: “the answers given”; - “lists” are replaced by the words: “score sheets”; - “different lists and names” are replaced by the words: “contradictory answers”; - “the same list, the same pair of candidates or the same candidate” are replaced by the words: “the same answer”; 2° For the application of the first paragraph of Article L. 66, the words: "for candidates or for third parties" are replaced by the words: ", as well as ballot papers other than those provided by the State " ; 3° For the application of the third paragraph of Article L. 66, after the words: “these ballots”, the words: “and envelopes” are added.
L. 123-30 Ordinance No. 2016-488 of April 21, 2016 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The criminal provisions provided for in Chapter VII of Title I of Book I of the electoral code are applicable to the consultation ballot, with the exception of articles L. 88-1 L. 90-1 L. 113-1
L. 123-31 Ordinance No. 2016-488 of April 21, 2016 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf A census commission is established, sitting in the most populated commune of the territorial jurisdiction where the consultation is organized and composed of three magistrates. Section 4: Miscellaneous provisions
L. 123-32 Ordinance No. 2016-488 of April 21, 2016 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The regularity of the consultation governed by this chapter may be contested under the conditions, forms and deadlines prescribed for complaints against the election of members of municipal councils.

L. 124-1 ORDER no. 2015-1341 of October 23, 2015 - art. 3 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The right of any person to access information relating to the environment held, received or established by the public authorities mentioned in article
L. 124-3 or on their behalf is exercised under the conditions defined by the provisions of Title I of Book III of the Code of Relations between the Public and the Administration, subject to the provisions of this chapter. service-public.fr > Access to administrative documents: Right of access to information relating to the environment
L. 124-2 LAW n°2021-1308 of October 8, 2021 - art. 36 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf Is considered information relating to the environment within the meaning of this chapter any information available, whatever the medium, concerning: 1° The state of the elements of the environment, in particular the air, the atmosphere, water, soil, land, landscapes, natural sites, coastal or marine areas and biological diversity, as well as the interactions between these elements; 2° Decisions, activities and factors, including substances, energy, noise, radiation, waste, emissions, spills and other discharges, likely to have an impact on the state of the elements covered in 1°, as well as the decisions and activities intended to protect these elements; 3° The state of human health, the safety and living conditions of people, buildings and cultural heritage, to the extent that they are or can be altered by elements of the environment, decisions, activities or the factors mentioned above; 4° The cost and benefit analyzes as well as the economic hypotheses used in the context of the decisions and activities referred to in 2°; 5° Reports drawn up by public authorities or on their behalf on the application of legislative and regulatory provisions relating to the environment.
L. 124-3 Law n°2005-1319 of October 26, 2005 - art. 2 () JORF October 27, 2005 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf Any person who requests it receives information relating to the environment held by: 1° The State, local authorities and their groups, public establishments; 2° Persons entrusted with a public service mission relating to the environment, to the extent that this information concerns the exercise of this mission. Bodies or institutions acting in the exercise of jurisdictional or legislative powers are not subject to the provisions of this chapter.
L. 124-4 ORDER no. 2015-1341 of October 23, 2015 - art. 3 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I. - After assessing the interest of a communication, the public authority may reject the request for information relating to the environment whose consultation or communication harms: 1° The interests mentioned in articles L. 311-5
L. 311-8 of the code of relations between the public and the administration, with the exception of those referred to in e and h of 2° of the article
L. 311-5; 2° To the protection of the environment to which it relates; 3° To the interests of the natural person having provided, without being obliged to do so by a legislative or regulatory provision or by an act of an administrative or jurisdictional authority, the requested information without consenting to its disclosure; p.60 Environmental Code L. 124-5 Law n°2005-1319 of October 26, 2005 - art. 2 () JORF October 27, 2005 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I.-When a public authority receives a request relating to information relating to the factors mentioned in 2° of the article
L. 124-2, it indicates to its author, if he requests it, the address where he can find out about the processes and methods used for the preparation of the data. II.-The public authority may only reject the request for information relating to emissions of substances into the environment in the case where its consultation or communication harms: 1° The conduct of the foreign policy of the France, public security or national defense; 2° The conduct of legal proceedings or the investigation of offenses which may give rise to criminal sanctions; 3° Has intellectual property rights.
L. 124-6 ORDER no. 2015-1341 of October 23, 2015 - art. 3 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I.-The rejection of a request for information relating to the environment is notified to the applicant by a reasoned written decision specifying the avenues and deadlines for appeal. The article
L. 232-4 of the code of relations between the public and the administration does not apply. II.-When this rejection is based on 1° of II of article
L. 124-4, this decision indicates the deadline within which the document will be completed, as well as the public authority responsible for its preparation. When this rejection is based on 2° of II of article
L. 124-4, this decision indicates, where applicable, the public authority holding this information. A request cannot be rejected on the basis of 3° of II of article
L. 124-4 only after the public authority has previously invited the applicant to specify it and helped him to this end.
L. 124-7 Law n°2005-1319 of October 26, 2005 - art. 2 () JORF October 27, 2005 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I. - Public authorities take measures allowing the public to know their rights of access to information relating to the environment that they hold, and ensure that the public can access the information sought. To this end, they establish directories or lists of categories of information relating to the environment in their possession, accessible free of charge and indicating the place where this information is made available to the public. II. - Public authorities ensure that information relating to the environment collected by them or on their behalf is precise and kept up to date and can give rise to comparison. They organize the conservation of this information in order to allow its dissemination electronically.
L. 124-8 Law n°2005-1319 of October 26, 2005 - art. 2 () JORF October 27, 2005 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf A decree of the Council of State, taken after the opinion of the commission for access to administrative documents, specifies the terms of application of this chapter. It defines the categories of information relating to the environment which must be the subject of public dissemination within a deadline it sets. It determines the modalities according to which the State and the local authorities, each as far as it is concerned, make available to the public the lists of public establishments and other persons mentioned in article
L. 124-3 which are attached to them or over which they exercise control. service-public.fr p.61 Environmental Code L. 125-1 ORDER no. 2015-1341 of October 23, 2015 - art. 3 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I.-Everyone has the right to be informed about the harmful effects on human health and the environment of the collection, transport, treatment, storage and deposit of waste as well as the measures taken to prevent or compensate for these effects. II.-This right consists in particular of: 1° The communication by the operator of a waste elimination installation of documents established within the framework of the provisions of Chapter I of Title I of Book V, making it possible to measure the effects of its activity on public health and the environment and outlining the measures taken to eliminate or reduce the harmful effects of waste; 2° The creation, on any waste elimination or storage site, at the initiative, either of the prefect, or of the municipal council of the municipality of establishment or of a neighboring municipality, of the commission mentioned in article
L. 125-2 -1; the prefect, who chairs the commission, has at its request the control operations that it deems necessary for its work, within the framework of Title I or Title IV (Chapter I) of Book V; the documents established by the operator of a waste elimination facility to measure the effects of its activity on public health and the environment are transmitted to the commission; the establishment and operating costs of the commission are covered by the group provided for in article
L. 541-43, when it exists; 3° The establishment, by municipalities or public establishments of intermunicipal cooperation or mixed unions referred to in article
L. 2224-13 of the general code of local authorities and by the prefects, documents allowing the evaluation of the measures taken to eliminate the waste for which they are responsible; these documents can be freely consulted. III.-A decree of the Council of State defines the conditions for exercising this right. It determines in particular the methods by which this information is made known to the public. IV.-The provisions contained in this article apply without prejudice to the provisions of Book III of the code of relations between the public and the administration.
L. 125-2 LAW n°2021-1520 of November 25, 2021 - art. 10 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I.-Everyone has a right to information on the major risks to which they are subject in certain areas of the territory and on the safeguard measures which concern them. This right applies to technological risks and foreseeable natural risks. In this context, elements subject to rules for the protection of national defense secrecy or necessary to safeguard the interests of national defense or likely to facilitate acts likely to be facilitated may not be communicated or made available to the public. to harm public health, safety or sanitation or whose disclosure would be likely to infringe manufacturing secrets or business secrets. II.-The State and municipalities exposed to at least one major risk contribute to the information provided for in I by making the information available to them available to the public. II bis.-In municipalities exposed to at least one major risk, the mayor communicates to the population, by any appropriate means, the characteristics of the major risks, prevention measures, terms L. 125-2 -1 LAW n°2021-1520 of November 25, 2021 - art. 10 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The State representative in the department may create, on his initiative or at the request of the operator, communities or local residents, around one or more installations classified for environmental protection subject to authorization in application of the article
L. 512-1 or in geographical areas involving industrial and technological risks and pollution, a site monitoring commission when the nuisances, dangers and inconveniences presented by this or these installations or in these geographical areas, with regard to the interests protected by the article
L. 511-1, justify it. This decision is taken after consultation with the competent advisory commission. The establishment and operating costs of the commission are covered by the State, unless there is a specific agreement between the actors or in cases where funding is provided for by law. This commission can call on the skills of recognized experts, in particular to carry out third-party assessments. It is kept informed of any incident or accident affecting the safety of the installations around which it is located. It is provided by the State with the means to fulfill its mission. The conditions of application of this article and in particular the rules of composition and operation of the commission are set by decree in the Council of State.
L. 125-2 -2 LAW n°2021-1104 of August 22, 2021 - art. 250 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf Agents of the State and local authorities who contribute to the knowledge and prediction of evolving or dangerous natural phenomena, particularly in the context of the development of the documents constituting the information for purchasers or tenants mentioned in the article
L. 125-5, may carry out the observation of all places in which natural phenomena are in progress or likely to occur and endanger the lives of populations, by means of cameras and sensors installed on aircraft circulating without no one on board and operated by a remote pilot. This observation can lead to the capture, recording and transmission of images as well as physical data. When these operations lead to the overview of private spaces, all precautions are taken to limit the collection of personal data concerning these private spaces. Registration is not permanent and is only made possible in cases exhaustively listed by the decree provided for in the last paragraph of this article, which are the subject of a customary doctrine disseminated by the minister responsible for registration. 'environment. These recordings are only received by agents, duly trained and authorized, who need to know them in order to carry out the missions mentioned in the first paragraph. When they contain personal data, these recordings or the personal data they contain are deleted after six months. The new technologies thus implemented have no impact on the exercise of the rights of the persons concerned provided for by Law No. 78-17 of January 6, 1978 relating to information technology. ique, L. 125-3 Ordinance no. 2021-1325 of October 13, 2021 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf Everyone has the right to be informed about the effects that the deliberate release of genetically modified organisms within the meaning of Title III of Book V may have for public health or the environment, while respecting the confidentiality of protected information. by the law. Subject to information recognized as confidential pursuant to article
L. 535-3, evaluation reports, decisions to authorize or refuse authorization, opinions from the National Agency for Health, Food, Environment and Work Safety as well as the Decisions of the community authority, in the event that an objection has been formulated by a Member State or the European Commission, are made public at the end of the authorization procedure. The results of observations carried out pursuant to the monitoring obligations are also made public. The information made public is grouped in a register accessible electronically and with the administrative authority competent to issue authorizations.
L. 125-4 Order No. 2000-914 of September 18, 2000 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The right to information on air quality and its effects on health and the environment is recognized for everyone throughout the country. The State is the guarantor of the exercise of this right, of the reliability of information and its dissemination. This right is exercised according to the terms defined in section 2 of chapter I of title II of book II.
L. 125-5 LAW n°2021-1104 of August 22, 2021 - art. 236 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I. - Purchasers or tenants of real estate located in areas covered by a technological risk prevention plan, by a mining risk prevention plan or by a foreseeable natural risk prevention plan, prescribed or approved, in seismic zones or in areas with radon potential defined by regulation or in an area likely to be reached by the retreat of the coastline defined in application of articles L. 121-22 L. 121-22 L. 121-22
L. 121-22 -7 of the town planning code, are informed by the seller or the lessor of the existence of these risks. For this purpose, a risk report is established. I bis.-Any announcement, whatever its distribution medium, relating to the sale of real estate requiring a risk assessment in accordance with I includes a statement specifying the means of accessing the information mentioned to the same I concerning the good. In the event of sale of all or part of a building, the statement of risks is given to the potential buyer by the seller during the first visit to the building, if such a visit takes place. Without prejudice to the first two paragraphs of this I bis, the state of risks is: 1° Integrated into the technical diagnosis file provided for in article
L. 271-4 of the Construction and Housing Code or, when the sale concerns an unbuilt building, annexed to the promise of sale or, in the absence of a promise, to the authentic deed of sale; 2° Annexed to the authentic deed of sale and, where applicable, to the preliminary contract, in the event of sale in the future state of completion. When the statement of risks is not given to the purchaser at the latest on the date of signing the promise of sale or the preliminary contract, the withdrawal period provided for in article
L. 271-1 of the same code only runs from the day after communication of this document to the purchaser. When the authentic deed of sale is not preceded by a promise of sale or a preliminary contract and the statement of risks is not attached to the authentic deed of sale, the reflection period mentioned in same article
L. 271-1 only runs from the day after communication of this document to the purchaser. p.64 Environmental Code http://legifrance.gouv.fr/affichTexte.do?cidTexte=JORFTEXT000044201935 II.-Any announcement, whatever its distribution medium, relating to the rental of real estate requiring a risk assessment in accordance with I of this article includes a statement specifying the means of access the information mentioned in the same I concerning the property. In the event of renting all or part of a building, the statement of risks is provided to the potential tenant by the lessor during the first visit to the building, if such a visit takes place. Without prejudice to the second paragraph of this II, upon conclusion of the lease, the statement of risks is annexed to the rental contract, under the conditions and according to the modalities provided for in Article 3-3 of Law No. 89- 462 of July 6, 1989 tending to improve rental relations and amending Law No. 86-1290 of December 23, 1986, or commercial leases mentioned in articles L. 145-1
L. 145-2 of the Commercial Code. III. # (Repealed). IV. # When a built building has suffered an incident giving rise to the payment of compensation in accordance with article L. 125-2
L. 128-2 of the Insurance Code, the seller or lessor of the building is required to inform, in the state of risks mentioned in I, I bis and II of this article, the purchaser or lessee of any disaster occurring during the period when he owned the building or of which he himself was informed in accordance with these provisions. When a building is subject to the obligations provided for in article
L. 121-22 -5 of the town planning code, the seller or lessor of the building is required to inform the purchaser or tenant in the statement of risks mentioned in I, I bis and II of this article. V. # In the event of non-compliance with I, the third to fifth paragraphs of I bis, the last paragraph of II and IV of this article, the purchaser or tenant may continue to terminate the contract or ask the judge for a decrease in price. VI. # A decree of the Council of State establishes the conditions of application of this article. VII. # This article is not applicable to the agreements mentioned in articles L. 323-14

L. 125-6 LAW n°2021-1104 of August 22, 2021 - art. 223 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I. # The State develops, based on the information available to it, information sectors on soils which include lands where knowledge of soil pollution justifies, particularly in the event of a change of use within the meaning of the article
L. 556-1 A, carrying out soil studies and pollution management measures to preserve public safety, health or sanitation and the environment. II. # The State representative in the department collects the opinions of the mayors of the municipalities in whose territory the soil information sector projects are located and, where applicable, that of the presidents of the competent public intermunicipal cooperation establishments in matters of town planning. He informs the owners of the land concerned. The information sectors on soils are decided by the State representative in the department. III. # The soil information sectors are indicated on one or more graphic documents and annexed to the local town planning plan or to the town planning document taking its place or to the municipal map. IV. # The State publishes, based on the information available to it, a map of former industrial sites and service activities. The planning certificate provided for in article
L. 410-1 of the town planning code indicates whether the land is located on a site listed on this map or on a former industrial or service site of which the department issuing the town planning certificate is aware. L. 125-7 LAW n°2021-1104 of August 22, 2021 - art. 223 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf Without prejudice to article L. 514-20
L. 125-5, when land located in the soil information sector mentioned in article
L. 125-6 is the subject of a sale or rental contract, the seller or lessor of the land is required to inform the purchaser or lessee in writing. It communicates the information made public by the State, in application of article
L. 125-6. The deed of sale or rental attests to the completion of this formality. Failing this and if noted pollution makes the land unsuitable for the purpose specified in the contract or, failing that, the use envisaged within the meaning of article
L. 556-1 A, within two years from the discovery of the pollution, the purchaser or tenant has the choice of requesting the termination of the contract or, depending on the case, having part of the sale price or obtain a reduction in rent. The buyer can also request the rehabilitation of the land at the seller's expense when the cost of this rehabilitation does not appear disproportionate to the sale price. The rehabilitation of the land is understood within the meaning of the same article
L. 556-1 A. A decree of the Council of State defines the terms of application of this article.
L. 125-8 Ordinance No. 2016-1058 of August 3, 2016 - art. 2 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The State representative in the department may create bodies to monitor the implementation of measures intended to avoid, reduce and, where possible, compensate for significant negative effects on the environment of infrastructure projects. linear subject to environmental assessment in application of article
L. 122-1. These bodies bring together the public administrations concerned, economic players, representatives of representative trade union organizations and chambers of agriculture, local authorities, approved environmental protection associations concerned as well as, where applicable, consumer representatives. and users, qualified individuals because of their skills in environmental protection or risk prevention. The State representative in the department may charge linear infrastructure operators with any study or expertise costs. A decree of the Council of State sets out the terms of application of this article.
L. 125-9 Ordinance No. 2012-6 of January 5, 2012 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf 1. Leases concluded or renewed relating to premises of more than 2,000 square meters for office or commercial use include an environmental annex. A decree defines the content of this annex. 2. The lessee and the lessor communicate to each other all useful information relating to the energy consumption of the rented premises. The lessee allows the lessor access to the rented premises for carrying out work to improve energy performance. 3. This environmental annex may provide for the obligations imposed on lessees to limit the energy consumption of the premises concerned. 4. These provisions take effect on January 1, 2012 with respect to leases entered into or renewed from that date. They take effect three years after the entry into force of Law No. 2010-788 of July 12, 2010 establishing a national commitment to the environment for current leases. service-public.fr > Commercial L. 125-10 Ordinance No. 2016-128 of February 10, 2016 - art. 19 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf Without prejudice to the provisions of article
L. 124-1, any person has the right to obtain, from him, information held by: 1° The operator of a basic nuclear installation; 2° The person responsible for the transport of radioactive substances, when the quantities exceed thresholds above which, in application of international conventions and regulations governing the transport of dangerous goods, the transport code and the texts adopted for their application, this transport is subject to the issuance, by the Nuclear Safety Authority or by a foreign authority competent in the field of the transport of radioactive substances, of an approval of the transport package model or a shipping approval , including under special arrangement. This information, whether received or established by them, relates to the risks or inconveniences that the installation or transport may present for the interests mentioned in article
L. 593-1 and on the measures taken to prevent or reduce these risks or disadvantages, under the conditions defined in articles L. 124-1 L. 124-6
L. 125-11 Ordinance No. 2012-6 of January 5, 2012 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf Disputes relating to refusals to communicate information opposed pursuant to article
L. 125-10 are brought before the administrative jurisdiction in accordance with the procedures provided for by Law No. 78-753 of July 17, 1978 relating to various measures to improve relations between the administration and the public and various administrative provisions, social and fiscal. The provisions of Chapter II of Title I of this law are not applicable to information communicated on the basis of article
L. 125-10. Subsection 2: Transparency in nuclear matters
L. 125-12 Ordinance No. 2012-6 of January 5, 2012 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf Transparency in nuclear matters consists of all the measures taken to guarantee the public's right to reliable and accessible information in matters of nuclear security as defined in article L. 591-1
L. 125-13 Ordinance No. 2016-128 of February 10, 2016 - art. 42 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The State ensures that the public is informed of the risks linked to nuclear activities defined in 1° of the article
L. 1333-1 of the public health code and their impact on the health and safety of people as well as on the environment. It is responsible for informing the public on the methods and results of monitoring nuclear safety and radiation protection as defined in article
L. 591-1. L. 125-14 Ordinance No. 2012-6 of January 5, 2012 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf Persons carrying out nuclear activities mentioned in the article
L. 125-13 must in particular respect the right of every person to be informed about the risks linked to nuclear activities and their impact on the health and safety of people and on the environment as well as on the releases of effluent from installations, under the conditions defined by this subsection and Chapters I to III, V and VI of Title IX of Book V and the decrees taken for their application.
L. 125-15 Ordinance No. 2016-128 of February 10, 2016 - art. 19 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf Any operator of a basic nuclear installation prepares each year a report which contains information concerning: 1° The measures taken to prevent or limit the risks and inconveniences that the installation may present for the interests mentioned in article
L. 593-1; 2° Incidents and accidents, subject to the reporting obligation pursuant to article
L. 591-5, occurring within the perimeter of the installation as well as the measures taken to limit their development and the consequences on the health of people and the environment; 3° The nature and results of measurements of radioactive and non-radioactive releases from the installation into the environment; 4° The nature and quantity of waste stored within the perimeter of the installation as well as the measures taken to limit the volume and the effects on health and the environment, in particular on soil and water.
L. 125-16 Ordinance No. 2012-6 of January 5, 2012 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The report mentioned in the article
L. 125-15 is submitted to the health, safety and working conditions committee of the basic nuclear installation, which can make recommendations. These recommendations are, where appropriate, appended to the document for publication and transmission purposes. The report is made public. It is transmitted to the local information commission provided for in subsection 3 and to the High Committee for Transparency and Information on Nuclear Security provided for in subsection 4 of this section.
L. 125-16 -1 LAW no. 2015-992 of August 17, 2015 - art. 123 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf People domiciled or established within the scope of a particular intervention plan mentioned in article
L. 741-6 of the internal security code defined for a basic nuclear installation regularly receive, without having to request it, information on the nature of the accident risks and on the envisaged consequences, on the perimeter of the specific intervention plan and on the security measures and the conduct to be followed in application of this plan. These information actions are subject to consultation by the local information commission provided for in article
L. 125-17 of this code and are carried out at the expense of the operators. Subsection 3: Local information commissions

participation This commission is responsible for a general mission of monitoring, information and consultation in matters of nuclear safety, radiation protection and the impact of nuclear activities on people and the environment with regard to site facilities. It ensures wide dissemination of the results of its work in a form accessible to as many people as possible. It organizes, at least once a year, a public meeting open to all. It can take up any subject falling within the skills mentioned in the second paragraph. has.
L. 125-18 Ordinance No. 2012-6 of January 5, 2012 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf A single local information commission can be created for several nearby basic nuclear installations, or one commission per site on which a basic nuclear installation has been established.
L. 125-19 Ordinance No. 2012-6 of January 5, 2012 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf A local information commission can be created as soon as a basic nuclear installation has been the subject of a creation authorization request pursuant to article L. 593-7
L. 125-20 LAW n°2015-992 of August 17, 2015 - art. 123 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I. # The local information commission includes: 1° Members of departmental councils, municipal councils or deliberative assemblies of interested municipal groups and regional councils; 2° Members of Parliament elected in the department; 3° Representatives of environmental protection associations, economic interests and representative employee union organizations as well as medical professions; 4° Qualified personalities. II. # Representatives of the Nuclear Safety Authority, other State services concerned and the regional health agency as well as representatives of the operator may attend, in an advisory capacity, the committee's meetings. They have full access to its work. III.-If the site is located in a border department, the composition of the commission mentioned in I is supplemented to include members from foreign states.
L. 125-21 LAW n°2013-403 of May 17, 2013 - art. 1 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The local information commission is created by decision of the president of the departmental council of the department over which the perimeter of the basic nuclear installation or installations extends or by joint decision of the presidents of the departmental councils if the perimeter extends across several departments. The president of the departmental council appoints the members of the commission. The commission is chaired by the president of the departmental council or by a local elected representative of the department appointed by him from among its members.
L. 125-22 Ordinance No. 2016-128 of February 10, 2016 - art. 17 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf If the scope of the basic nuclear installation includes a waste elimination or storage installation, the commission provided for in this subsection replaces the site monitoring commission mentioned in Article L. 125- 2
L. 125-23 Ordinance No. 2012-6 of January 5, 2012 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The local information commission can be given legal personality and take on the status of association governed by the law of January 1, 1901 relating to the association contract. p.69 L. 125-24 ORDER no. 2015-1341 of October 23, 2015 - art. 3 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf For the exercise of its missions, the local information commission can have expert assessments carried out, including epidemiological studies, and carry out any measurement or analysis in the environment relating to emissions or discharges from the site's installations. The operator, the Nuclear Safety Authority and other State services provide it with all the documents and information necessary to carry out its missions. Depending on the case, the provisions of articles L. 125-10
L. 125-11 or those of Chapter IV of Title II of Book I and Book III of the Code of Relations between the Public and the Administration are applicable to this communication.
L. 125-25 Ordinance No. 2012-6 of January 5, 2012 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The local information commission is informed by the operator of the requests addressed to it on the basis of the provisions of article
L. 125-10 within eight days of their receipt. Under the same conditions, the operator sends him the responses to these requests. The operator informs the commission of any incident or accident mentioned in article
L. 591-5 as soon as possible.
L. 125-25 -1 LAW no. 2015-992 of August 17, 2015 - art. 123 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf At the request of the president of the local information commission, the operator organizes a visit to the installation for its members in order to present to them its operation. In the event of an event of level greater than or equal to 1 on the international nuclear event classification scale, as soon as normal safety conditions are restored, the operator organizes for the attention of the members of the local information commission, at the request of its president, a visit to the installation in order to present to them the circumstances of the event as well as the measures taken to remedy it and limit its effects.
L. 125-26 LAW n°2015-992 of August 17, 2015 - art. 123 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The Nuclear Safety Authority, the ministers responsible for nuclear safety or radiation protection may consult the local information commission on any project concerning the perimeter of the basic nuclear installation. Consultation of the commission is obligatory for any project subject to a public inquiry as long as it is regularly constituted. Any modification of the specific intervention plan mentioned in article
L. 741-6 of the internal security code defined for a basic nuclear installation is the subject of consultation by the local information commission.
L. 125-27 Ordinance No. 2012-6 of January 5, 2012 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The local information commission may refer any question relating to nuclear safety and radiation protection affecting the site where it was established to the Nuclear Safety Authority and the ministers responsible for nuclear safety or radiation protection.
L. 125-28 Ordinance No. 2012-6 of January 5, 2012 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The local information commission may be contacted for an opinion on any question falling within its area of ​​competence by the departmental commission competent in matters of environment, health and technological risks mentioned in article

L. 125-29 Ordinance No. 2012-6 of January 5, 2012 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The local information commission and the High Committee for Transparency and Information on Nuclear Security provided for in subsection 4 of this section communicate to each other all information useful to the exercise of their missions and contribute to actions common information.
L. 125-30 Ordinance No. 2012-6 of January 5, 2012 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf Representatives designated by the health, safety and working conditions committee of an establishment comprising one or more of the basic nuclear installations mentioned in article
L. 593-2 are heard at their request by the local information commission each time they consider it necessary. The commission can also hear them on its own initiative.
L. 125-31 Ordinance No. 2012-6 of January 5, 2012 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I. # The expenses of the local information commission are financed by: 1° The State; 2° Local authorities and their groups. II. # If the commission has legal personality, in addition to the subsidies which may be allocated to it by the State, by these communities and by these groups, it may receive part of the proceeds of the tax established by article 43 of the finance law for 2000 (no. 99-1172 of December 30, 1999). III. # The accounts of the commission are subject to the control of the regional chamber of accounts.
L. 125-32 Ordinance No. 2012-6 of January 5, 2012 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The local information commissions can form a federation among themselves, taking the legal form of an association governed by the law of January 1, 1901 relating to the association contract, responsible for representing them before the national and European authorities and provide assistance to the committees on matters of common interest. The resources of this federation come in particular from subsidies paid by the State and contributions from the commissions which are members.
L. 125-33 Ordinance No. 2012-6 of January 5, 2012 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf A decree of the Council of State determines the terms of application of this subsection. It defines the clauses that must appear in the statutes of commissions with legal personality. Subsection 4: The High Committee for Transparency and Information on Nuclear Security
L. 125-34 Ordinance No. 2012-6 of January 5, 2012 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The High Committee for Transparency and Information on Nuclear Security is a body for information, consultation and debate on the risks linked to nuclear activities and the impact of these activities on human health, on the environment and nuclear security. As such, the high committee can issue an opinion on any question in these areas as well as on the controls and information relating to them. It may also address any question relating to the accessibility of information relating to nuclear security and propose any measure likely to guarantee or improve the transparency defined in article

L. 126-1 Ordinance No. 2016-1058 of August 3, 2016 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf When a public works, development or structure project has been the subject of a public inquiry pursuant to Chapter III of this Title, the State authority or the deliberative body of the community territorial authority or the public establishment responsible for the project decides, through a project declaration, on the general interest of the planned operation. The project declaration mentions the object of the operation as it appears in the file submitted to the investigation and includes the reasons and considerations which justify its character of general interest. The project declaration takes into consideration the impact study, the opinions of the environmental authority and the local authorities and their groups consulted in application of Article V
L. 122-1 and the result of the public consultation. It indicates, where applicable, the nature and reasons for the main modifications which, without altering the general scheme, are made to the project in view of the results of the public inquiry. In addition, it includes the elements mentioned in I of the article
L. 122-1 -1 of the environmental code. If the project declaration has not been made within one year from the closure of the investigation, the operation cannot be carried out without a new investigation. In the absence of a project declaration, no work authorization can be issued. If the work has not started to be executed within five years from the publication of the project declaration, the declaration becomes obsolete. However, in the absence of a change in the factual or legal circumstances, the deadline may be extended once for the same duration, without further investigation, by a project declaration taken in the same form as the initial declaration and occurring before the expiration of the five-year period. The project declaration is published under conditions defined by decree in the Council of State. Chapter VII: Geographic information infrastructure Section 1: General provisions

Juricaf This chapter applies, without prejudice to the provisions of Chapter IV of Title II of Book I, to series of geographical data: # held by a public authority, or on its behalf; p.73
