https://juricaf.org/recherche/article%20L.%20127-1%20%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22/facet_pays%3AFrance?tri=DESC Legislative part - Book I: Common provisions - Title II: Information and participation of citizens # in electronic format; # relating to an area in which France holds or exercises this is his competence; # and concerning one or more themes listed in Annexes I, II and III of Directive 2007/2/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council of 14 March 2007 establishing a geographic information infrastructure in the European Community (INSPIRE). For the purposes of this chapter, is considered to be: 1° “Geographic information infrastructure”, metadata, geographic data series and geographic data services; networked services and technologies; agreements on sharing, access and use; and coordination and monitoring mechanisms, processes and procedures established, operated or made available in accordance with this chapter; 2° “Geographic data”, any data directly or indirectly referring to a location; specific or a geographical area; 3° “Geographic data series”, an identifiable compilation of geographical data; 4° “Geographic data services”, the operations which can be carried out using a computer application on the geographical data contained in data series geographic or related metadata; 5° “Geographic object”, an abstract representation of a real phenomenon linked to a specific place or a geographical area; 6° “Metadata”, the information describing the series and services of geographical data and making their search, their inventory and their use; 7° “Interoperability”, the possibility of a combination of geographic data series and interaction of services, without repetitive manual intervention so that the result is consistent and the added value of the data series and services is reinforced; 8° “INSPIRE Portal”, a website or equivalent which provides access to the services referred to in the article
L. 127-4; 9° “Public authority”, the public authorities mentioned in article
L. 124-3 or any person acting on their behalf; 10° “Third party”, any natural or legal person other than a public authority within the meaning of 9°. When several identical copies of the same series of spatial data are held by or on behalf of several public authorities, this chapter applies only to the reference version from which the different copies are taken. This chapter also applies to geographic data services which concern data contained in the geographic data series referred to in the first paragraph, as well as to geographic data series and services held by a third party to which the network mentioned in Article
L. 127-4 was made available in accordance with article
L. 127-5. However, this chapter is only applicable to series of geographic data held by a municipality or on behalf of it only if legislative provisions require their collection or dissemination. Section 2: Metadata
L. 127-2 Ordinance No. 2010-1232 of October 21, 2010 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf Public authorities create and update metadata for the geographic data series and services defined in Article
L. 127-1 in accordance with the terms of application defined in Regulation (EC) No. 1205/2008 of December 3, 2008. Juricaf Public authorities implement data series and services in accordance with the technical modalities of interoperability determined by the regulations adopted pursuant to Directive 2007/2/EC of March 14, 2007. Section 4: Network Services

Juricaf I. # Public authorities shall establish and operate a network of the following services relating to spatial data sets and services for which metadata have been created in accordance with this Chapter: (a) Search services to identify spatial data sets and services on the basis of the corresponding metadata content and display the metadata content; b) Consultation services allowing at least the display of data, navigation, changing scale, opting for a panoramic view, or superimposing several series of searchable data and displaying legends as well as any relevant content metadata; (c) Download services enabling copies of geographic data sets or parts thereof to be downloaded and, where possible, accessed directly; (d) Transformation services to transform geographic data sets to achieve interoperability; e) Services for calling geographic data services. These services take into account user requirements in this area, are easy to use and accessible to the public via the Internet. Sharing of data between public authorities
L. 127-8 Ordinance No. 2010-1232 of October 21, 2010 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I. # Public authorities may access, share, exchange and use geographic data series and services, within the meaning of this chapter, held by other public authorities, for the purposes of carrying out a public service mission in relation to the environment, to the extent that these geographic data series and services concern the exercise of this mission. However, the provisions of this section do not apply to public authorities when they exercise a public service mission of an industrial or commercial nature, nor to series and services of e geographical data produced or received by public authorities in the exercise of such a mission. II. # Any restriction likely to create practical obstacles, at the point of use, to access and sharing of these geographic data sets and services between public authorities is prohibited. The terms and conditions for making geographic data series and services available are determined by decree of the Council of State. III. # Access and sharing of geographic data series and services between public authorities, within the meaning of this section, are also open to public authorities of other Member States, as well as, according to the principle of reciprocity and equal treatment, to organs s established by international agreements to which the European Union and the Member States are parties, for the purposes of carrying out a public service mission relating to the environment, insofar as these geographic data series and services concern the exercise of this mission. Access by Community institutions and bodies to public authorities' geographic data series and services is set out in Regulation (EU) No. 268/2010 of March 29, 2010. IV. # Public authorities may limit access and sharing of geographic data series and services, within the meaning of this section, if this access or sharing is likely to harm one of the interests set out in II of the article L. 124-5
L. 127-9 ORDER no. 2015-1341 of October 23, 2015 - art. 3 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf Public authorities may request access or sharing of the geographic data series and services referred to in Article
L. 127-8 to a royalty or operating license under the conditions defined by the provisions of articles L. 323-1
L. 325-8 of the code of relations between the public and the administration, notwithstanding the provisions of the last paragraph of the article
L. 321-2 of the same code. Spatial data sets and services provided to European Union institutions and bodies for the fulfillment of reporting obligations arising from European environmental legislation are not subject to payment. A decree of the Council of State determines the conditions under which royalties are set and licenses are granted. Section 6: Miscellaneous provisions

L. 131-1 LAW n°2016-1087 of August 8, 2016 - art. 20 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf A public establishment of the State governed by this code may be attached to one or more public establishments of the State, at the request of two thirds of the members of its board of directors and after advice from the establishment(s) to which this connection is requested, in order to pool services and resources. The services and resources shared between establishments are specified by decree. In the event of attachment, the establishments retain their legal personality and their financial autonomy. Section 1: Environment and Energy Management Agency
L. 131-3 LAW n°2023-175 of March 10, 2023 - art. 54 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I.-The Environment and Energy Management Agency is a public establishment of the State of an industrial and commercial nature. II.-This public establishment carries out actions, in particular the orientation and animation of research, the provision of services, information and incentives in each of the following areas: 1° Prevention and fight against pollution air ; 2° Prevention of waste production, including the fight against food waste; Waste Management ; the transition to the circular economy; soil protection and restoration of polluted sites; 3° The redevelopment and monitoring of a final waste storage facility authorized after July 14, 1992, when these operations are made necessary due to a failure or insufficiency of the operator's guarantees; 4° Saving energy and raw materials and developing renewable energies, particularly of plant origin; 5° The development of clean and economical technologies; 6° The fight against noise pollution; 7° The fight against global warming and adaptation to climate change; 8° Statistical monitoring of agrivoltaic installations defined in article
L. 314-36 of the energy code. III.-The agency coordinates its actions with those carried out by water agencies in areas of common interest. IV.-To accomplish its missions, the agency has a delegation in each region. The representative of the State, as the case may be, in the regions, the community of Corsica, the communities governed by articles 73 or 74 of the Constitution and in New Caledonia is the territorial delegate of the agency. V.-The agency ensures monitoring and observation of extended producer responsibility sectors. The costs borne by the agency to carry out the mission mentioned in the first paragraph of this V are covered by a fee paid by the producers or their eco-organization, the amount of which is fixed by decree. The agency division carrying out these actions has financial autonomy within the limit of the proceeds of the contributions received. Its budget constitutes an annual budget branch of the agency. p.79 Environmental Code http://legifrance.gouv.fr/affichTexte.do?cidTexte=JORFTEXT000033016237 https://legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/article_lc/LEGIARTI000033033709/2024-05-14 https:// www.courdecassation.fr/recherche-judilibre?search_api_fulltext=%22L%20131-1%22+%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&judilibre_juridiction=cc&op=Rechercher+sur+judilibre https://www.courdecassation.fr/recherche -judilibre?search_api_fulltext=%22L%20131-1%22+%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&judilibre_juridiction=ca&op=Rechercher+sur+judilibre https://legifrance.gouv.fr/search/cetat?tab_selection=cetat&searchField= ALL&query=%22article%20L131-1%22%20du%20%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&page=1&init=true&dateDecision= https://juricaf.org/recherche/article%20L.%20131-1%20% 22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22/facet_pays%3AFrance?tri=DESC http://legifrance.gouv.fr/affichTexte.do?cidTexte=JORFTEXT000047294244 https://legifr ance.gouv.fr/codes/article_lc/LEGIARTI000047303590/2024-05-14 https://www.courdecassation.fr/recherche-judilibre?search_api_fulltext=%22L%20131-3%22+%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement %22&judilibre_juridiction=cc&op=Rechercher+sur+judilibre https://www.courdecassation.fr/recherche-judilibre?search_api_fulltext=%22L%20131-3%22+%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&judilibre_juridiction=ca&op=Rechercher+ sur+judilibre https://legifrance.gouv.fr/search/cetat?tab_selection=cetat&searchField=ALL&query=%22article%20L131-3%22%20du%20%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&page=1&init=true&dateDecision= https://juricaf.org/recherche/article%20L.%20131-3%20%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22/facet_pays%3AFrance?tri=DESC https://legifrance.gouv.fr/affichCodeArticle. do?cidTexte=LEGITEXT000023983208&idArticle=LEGIARTI000047298015&dateTexte=&categorieLink=cid Legislative part - Book I: Common provisions - Title III: Institutions
L. 131-4 LAW n°2022-217 of February 21, 2022 - art. 57 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The agency's board of directors is made up of: 1° Representatives of the State and the National Agency for Territorial Cohesion; 2° A deputy and a senator; 3° Representatives of local authorities and public intermunicipal cooperation establishments with their own taxation; 4° Qualified individuals, representatives of environmental protection associations approved under the article

L. 131-5 Ordinance No. 2004-637 of July 1, 2004 - art. 27 () JORF July 2, 2004 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The agency has a scientific council whose composition is decided jointly by the ministers responsible for the environment, research and industry.
L. 131-6 LAW n°2022-217 of February 21, 2022 - art. 57 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The agency can award subsidies and grant repayable advances. In particular, it may receive royalties on inventions and new processes to which it has contributed, royalties for services rendered and the proceeds from taxes. The agency delegates to the region, at the latter's request, the allocation of subsidies and financial assistance in terms of energy transition and circular economy provided for as part of its contribution to the State-Region plan contract. The agency cannot object to the delegation of an annual amount of subsidies and assistance amounting to a maximum of 75% of the average annual credits mobilized by the agency under the State-Region plan contract on the last three years. The agency and the voluntary region conclude a regional ecological transition agreement which defines the duration of the delegation, the amount of subsidies and assistance delegated to the region, the criteria for awarding aid, the objectives to be achieved as well as the modalities of settlement of charges relating to this delegation.

L. 131-6. Section 2: French Biodiversity Office
L. 131-8 LAW n°2019-773 of July 24, 2019 - art. 1 (VD) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf A public State establishment is created called: “French Biodiversity Office #.
L. 131-9 LAW n°2022-217 of February 21, 2022 - art. 152 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I.-The French Biodiversity Office contributes, with regard to terrestrial, aquatic and marine environments, to the monitoring, preservation, management and restoration of biodiversity as well as to the balanced and sustainable management of water in coordination with the national policy to combat global warming. //legifrance.gouv.fr/search/cetat?tab_selection=cetat&searchField=ALL&query=%22article%20L131-9%22%20du%20%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&page=1&init=true&dateDecision= https://juricaf .org/recherche/article%20L.%20131-9%20%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22/facet_pays%3AFrance?tri=DESC Legislative part - Book I: Common provisions - Title III: Institutions 1° Contribution to the exercise of administrative police and judicial police missions relating to water, natural spaces, species, hunting and fishing as well as health police missions linked to wildlife; 2° Development of knowledge, research and expertise on species, environments, their functionalities and uses, on ecosystem services, on the links between climate change and biodiversity as well as on health risks linked to wildlife savage. The office manages or coordinates information systems on biodiversity, water, aquatic environments and marine environments; 3° Expertise and assistance in assessing the state of wildlife and adaptive management of the species mentioned in Article THE
L. 425-16; 4° Support for the design, implementation and evaluation of water and biodiversity policies, particularly at the territorial level: a) Support for the State for the development of the national strategy for biodiversity defined in article
L. 110-3 and monitoring of its implementation; b) Contribution to the fight against biopiracy and monitoring of the system of access to genetic resources and fair and equitable sharing of the benefits arising from their use; c) Support for the implementation of the principle mentioned in 2° of II of the article
L. 110-1 and monitoring of measures to compensate for damage to biodiversity; d) Support for monitoring the implementation of European regulations and directives and international conventions as well as cooperation actions; e) Support for the State and its public establishments responsible for the management of water, biodiversity and natural spaces, particularly in terms of combating pressures on biodiversity and combating species invasive exotics, wildlife management, habitat improvement and land management practices; f) Support, in conjunction with basin committees, for local authorities, their groups and their public establishments responsible for the management of water, biodiversity and natural spaces, particularly in the fight against pressures which work on biodiversity, the fight against invasive exotic species, wildlife management, habitat improvement and land management practices; g) Support for socio-economic actors and environmental protection or environmental education associations in their actions in favor of biodiversity; h) Financial support, through the allocation of financial aid to projects in favor of biodiversity and sustainable and balanced management of e water resources and through the guarantee of financial solidarity between river basins; 5° Management, restoration and support for the management of natural areas, particularly coastal areas including coral reefs and associated ecosystems; 6° Communication, public awareness, support for mobilization and training: a) Support for citizen mobilization, civil society and economic sector stakeholders on biodiversity issues, particularly on the link between man and nature ; b) Training, particularly in police matters, and support for initial and continuing training actions, particularly in the context of national education, higher education, research and agricultural education; c) Contribution to the structuring of biodiversity and ecological services professions; On behalf of the State, he is responsible for organizing the examination of the hunting license as well as the issuance of the hunting license. II.-The intervention of the French Biodiversity Office covers all terrestrial, aquatic and marine environments of the metropolitan territory, the communities governed by article 73 of the Constitution, the communities of Saint-Martin and Saint-Pierre and Miquelon as well as the French Southern and Antarctic Lands. It can also lead , within the framework of conventions, actions in Saint-Barthélemy, in the Wallis and Futuna islands, in French Polynesia as well as in New Caledonia or in its provinces, at the request of these communities. III.-The office and local authorities coordinate their actions in areas of common interest. The regions or communities exercising the powers of the regions and the office may jointly set up, within the framework of an agreement signed between the parties, regional biodiversity agencies p.81 Environmental Code Legislative part - Book I: Common provisions - Title III: Institutions with which the departments and local authorities exercising the powers of the departments may be associated. These agencies carry out their missions within the scope of the office's missions, with the exception of police missions and the issuance of hunting licenses. IV. - The representative of the State, as the case may be, in the department, the community of Corsica or the community governed by articles 73 or 74 of the Constitution ensures, as territorial delegate of the office, the coherence of the exercise of the administrative police missions of water and the environment of the office in the territories under its jurisdiction with the actions of other services and public establishments of the State 
L. 131-10 LAW n°2019-773 of July 24, 2019 - art. 1 (VD) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The French Biodiversity Office is administered by a board of directors which includes: 1° A first college made up of representatives of the State, representatives of national public establishments working in the field of competence of the office and qualified personalities; 2° A second college comprising representatives of the economic sectors concerned, representatives of professional agricultural and forestry organizations, approved environmental protection associations, managers of natural areas, hunting authorities and fishing authorities for fun ; 3° A third college comprising representatives of the basin committees as well as local authorities and their groups; 4° A fourth college composed of elected representatives of the person nel of the office; 5° A fifth college composed of two deputies including one elected in an overseas constituency and two senators including one elected in an overseas constituency. Any parliamentarian who is a member of the board of directors of the French Biodiversity Office, designated because of his or her elective mandate, may be replaced by another parliamentarian from the same deliberative assembly. The ministers responsible for the environment and agriculture appoint a Government commissioner, who belongs to the first college. A decree specifies the conditions under which this Government commissioner can request the inclusion of an item on the agenda of the board of directors, cause the convening of an extraordinary board of directors or oppose a decision of the board of directors and request a new deliberation. Representatives of the National Hunters Federation, departmental federations ales of hunters and the National Federation of Fishing and Protection of the Aquatic Environment represent 10% of the members of the board of directors. This number of representatives is truncated to the unit. The board of directors is composed to include at least one representative from each of the five overseas ecosystem basins. It is composed in such a way that the gap between the number of men, on the one hand, and the number of women, on the other hand, is not greater than one. When an organization is called upon to designate more than one member of the board, it makes these designations in such a way that the difference between the number of men designated, on the one hand, and the number of women designated, on the other part, is not greater than one. The same rule applies to the designation of qualified personalities of the first college. The chairman of the board of directors is elected from within the board of directors by its members 
L. 131-11 LAW n°2019-773 of July 24, 2019 - art. 1 (VD) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The board of directors may delegate certain of its responsibilities, under conditions defined by decree, to the management boards of protected areas placed under the responsibility of the French Biodiversity Office. It may establish within itself specialized commissions and delegate certain of its responsibilities to them, under conditions defined by decree.

L. 131-12 LAW n°2019-773 of July 24, 2019 - art. 1 (VD) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf A steering committee bringing together representatives of the different parties concerned by the missions of the French Biodiversity Office defined in article
L. 131-9 is placed with the board of directors of the establishment, which determines its composition and operation. The board of directors may delegate certain of its powers to him.
L. 131-13 LAW n°2019-773 of July 24, 2019 - art. 1 (VD) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The French Biodiversity Office is headed by a director general, appointed by decree.
L. 131-14 LAW n°2019-773 of July 24, 2019 - art. 1 (VD) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The resources of the French Biodiversity Office consist of: 1° Subsidies and contributions from the State and its public establishments as well as, where applicable, managers of marine protected areas and local authorities and their groups; 2° Revenue from assigned taxes; 3° Any public or private subsidy; 4° Donations and legacies; 5° The proceeds from sales and services that it carries out within the framework of its missions; 6° Fees for services rendered; 7° The products of contracts and agreements; 8° Income from movable and immovable property; 9° The proceeds of alienations; 10° Generally speaking, all revenues authorized by laws and regulations, provided that they do not degrade the resources of water agencies.
L. 131-15 LAW n°2020-1721 of December 29, 2020 - art. 82 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The resources of the program entrusted to the French Biodiversity Office as part of the national action plan defined in article
L. 253-6 of the rural and maritime fishing code include the share of contribution mentioned in this respect in article 135 of finance law no. 2017-1837 for 2018 and are spent, for an amount at least equal, in the form of aid provided by the office under this program.
L. 131-16 LAW n°2020-1721 of December 29, 2020 - art. 82 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf As part of the implementation of the program mentioned in article
L. 131-15, the French Biodiversity Office directly or indirectly provides financial assistance to public or private persons.

L. 132-1 LAW n°2019-773 of July 24, 2019 - art. 21 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The Agency for the Environment and Energy Management, the National Forestry Office, the Conservatory of Coastal Space and Lake Shores, the French Biodiversity Office, national parks, agencies of water, the Center of National Monuments and the National Agency for the Management of Radioactive Waste may exercise the rights recognized to the civil party with regard to facts causing direct or indirect harm to the interests which they have as their object to defend and constitute an infringement of the legislative provisions relating to the protection of nature and the environment, the improvement of the living environment, the protection of water, air, soil, sites and landscapes, town planning or having as their object the fight against pollution and nuisances, as well as the texts taken for their application. Without prejudice to compensation for other damages suffered, the public legal entities mentioned in the preceding paragraph who intervened materially or financially are entitled to reimbursement, by the person(s) responsible, of the costs incurred by them. The chambers of agriculture, the regional natural parks, the National Center for Forest Property, the legal entities designated by the decree in the Council of State provided for in the first paragraph of the article
L. 412-10 to obtain the prior informed consent of communities of inhabitants and regularly declared associations carrying out activities in the field of conservation of traditional knowledge registered in their statutes for at least three years may also exercise the rights recognized to the civil party under the conditions defined above.
L. 132-2 LAW no. 2014-1170 of October 13, 2014 - art. 67 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The representative agricultural and forestry union organizations as well as the chambers of agriculture, the National Forestry Office and the National Forest Property Center are called upon within the framework of the laws and regulations in force to participate in the action of the public authorities in terms of environmental protection or land management, when it comes to rural areas.
L. 132-3 LAW n°2022-217 of February 21, 2022 - art. 191 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf Owners of real estate may conclude a contract with a public authority, a public establishment or a legal entity under private law acting for the protection of the environment with a view to incurring at their expense, as well as at the expense of the owners property, the real obligations that they see fit, as long as such obligations have as their aim the maintenance, conservation, management or restoration of elements of biodiversity or ecological functions. These bonds can be used for compensation purposes. The duration of the obligations, the reciprocal commitments and the possibilities for revision and termination must appear in the contract. The duration provided for in the contract cannot exceed ninety-nine years. Established in authentic form, the contract giving rise to the real obligation is not subject to registration fees and does not give rise to the collection of the land registration tax provided for, respectively, in articles 662 and 663 of the general code taxes. It also does not give rise to payment of the contribution provided for in article 879 of the same code. The owner who has granted a rural lease on his land can only, under penalty of absolute nullity, implement a real environmental obligation with the prior agreement of the lessee and subject to the rights of third parties. Lack of response to a request agreement within two months constitutes acceptance. Any refusal must be justified. The implementation of a real environmental obligation cannot in any way call into question the rights linked to the exercise of hunting, nor those relating to hunting reserves. p.84 Environmental Code Institutions Chapter II bis: High Council for the climate
L. 132-4 LAW n°2019-1147 of November 8, 2019 - art. 10 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I.-The High Council for the Climate, an independent body, is placed under the Prime Minister. In addition to its president, the High Council for the Climate includes at most twelve members chosen because of their scientific, technical and economic expertise in the fields of climate sciences and ecosystems, the reduction of greenhouse gas emissions as well as adaptation and resilience to climate change. The members of the High Climate Council are appointed by decree for a five-year term, renewable once. When a member ceases his functions, a new member is appointed, after advice from the president of the High Council for the Climate, for the duration of the mandate remaining to be established. The members of the High Climate Council cannot request or receive any instructions from the Government or any other public or private person in the exercise of their missions. The members of the High Council for the Climate send to the High Authority for the Transparency of Public Life a declaration of interests under the conditions provided for in III of Article 4 of Law No. 2013-907 of October 11, 2013 relating to Transparency in public life. II.-The High Council for the Climate produces a report each year which concerns in particular: 1° Compliance with the trajectory of reducing greenhouse gas emissions with regard to the carbon budgets defined in application of the article
L. 222-1 A of this code and the low-carbon strategy mentioned in article
L. 222-1 B; 2° The implementation and effectiveness of policies and measures decided by the State and local authorities to reduce greenhouse gas emissions, develop carbon sinks, reduce the carbon footprint and develop adaptation climate change, including budgetary and tax provisions; 3° The socio-economic impact, particularly on training and employment, and environmental impact, including on biodiversity, of these different public policies. In this report, the High Council puts France's commitments and actions into perspective in relation to those of other countries. It issues recommendations and proposals to improve France's action, the contributions of different sectors of economic activity to compliance with carbon budgets as well as the reduction of greenhouse gas emissions linked to international aeronautical and maritime transport. This report is submitted to the Prime Minister and transmitted to Parliament as well as to the Economic, Social and Environmental Council. The Government presents to Parliament and the Economic, Social and Environmental Council, within six months following the submission of this report, the measures already implemented and those planned in response to the recommendations and proposals in this report. It presents an explanation for each of the unachieved objectives as well as the means implemented to achieve them. The High Council issues an opinion on the national low-carbon strategy and carbon budgets as well as on the report mentioned in II of the article
L. 222-1 D. It assesses the coherence of the low-carbon strategy with respect to national policies and France's European and international commitments, in particular the Paris climate agreement and the pursued objective of achieving carbon neutrality in 2050, while taking into account the socio-economic impacts of the transition for households and businesses, sovereignty issues and environmental impacts. III.-The High Council for the Climate was created on November 27, 2018. IV.-The terms of organization and operation of the High Council are specified by decree. L. 132-5 LAW n°2019-1147 of November 8, 2019 - art. 10 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The High Council for the Climate may take action on its own initiative or be contacted by the Government, the President of the National Assembly, the President of the Senate or the President of the Economic, Social and Environmental Council to issue an opinion, with regard to of its competence, on a bill, a proposed law or a question relating to its area of ​​expertise. In this opinion, the High Council for the Climate studies the compatibility of the proposal or project with the carbon budgets of the national low-carbon strategy. Chapter III: National Council for Ecological Transition
L. 133-1 LAW n°2012-1460 of December 27, 2012 - art. 13 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The National Council for Ecological Transition is chaired by the minister responsible for ecology or his representative. It can decide to create permanent specialized training within it.
L. 133-2 LAW n°2018-699 of August 3, 2018 - art. 54 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The National Council for Ecological Transition is consulted on: 1° Bills concerning, primarily, the environment or energy; 2° National strategies relating to sustainable development, biodiversity and the development of corporate social and environmental responsibility and the low-carbon strategy. It can take up any question of national interest concerning the ecological transition and sustainable development or having an impact on them. It is informed each year by the Government of the evolution of national performance and sustainable development indicators relevant to measuring the progress of the ecological transition. Its members include three deputies and three senators, as well as two representatives to the European Parliament elected in France.
L. 133-3 LAW n°2012-1460 of December 27, 2012 - art. 13 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The opinions of the National Council for Ecological Transition are made available to the public electronically. They are transmitted electronically to Parliament, the Economic, Social and Environmental Council, regional economic, social and environmental councils as well as organizations interested in the ecological transition.
L. 133-4 LAW n°2012-1460 of December 27, 2012 - art. 13 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The composition and operating methods of the National Council for Ecological Transition, in particular, are specified by regulation. p.86 Environmental Code https://juricaf.org/recherche/article%20L.%20133-4%20%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22/facet_pays%3AFrance?tri=DESC Legislative part - Book I: Common provisions - Title III: Institutions Chapter IV: Institutions relating to biodiversity you
L. 134-1 LAW n°2019-773 of July 24, 2019 - art. 21 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The National Biodiversity Committee constitutes a body for information, exchange and consultation on strategic issues linked to biodiversity. To this end, it organizes regular consultations with other consultation and reflection bodies whose missions relate to biodiversity. It can be consulted by the Government on any subject relating to biodiversity or having a significant effect on it. He can also take action ex officio. The scope of the committee's advisory competence as well as its composition and the terms of its operation are specified by decree in the Council of State. He gives his opinion on the strategic orientations of the French Biodiversity Office. The National Biodiversity Committee is made up of representatives of local authorities and their groups, national public establishments working in the field of biodiversity, relevant socio-professional organizations, landowners, nature users, associations, organizations or foundations working for the preservation of biodiversity and managers of natural spaces, scientists or representatives of organizations research and qualified personalities. The composition of the National Biodiversity Committee ensures balanced representation of women and men. To this end, the proportion of members of each sex making up the committee cannot be less than 40%. The decree provided for in the second paragraph specifies the distribution by sex of the persons designated by each of the competent bodies and authorities and the adjustment methods necessary to respect this rule of balanced representation. The composition of the committee ensures the representation of each overseas department and community, taking into account, in particular, the richness of their biodiversity. e.
L. 134-2 LAW n°2016-1087 of August 8, 2016 - art. 14 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The National Nature Protection Council's mission is to provide, through its opinions, scientific and technical expertise. It can be consulted on draft laws, ordinances and decrees concerning its areas of competence and the related scientific and technical work. He can also take action ex officio. A Council of State decree specifies the skills, operating methods and composition of the National Nature Protection Council, as well as the conditions under which its composition contributes to a balanced representation of women and men, of a on the one hand, and to a balanced representation of the life sciences and the human sciences, on the other hand. It sets the transparency rules applicable to experts from the National Nature Protection Council. The composition of the National Nature Protection Council contributes to a significant representation of specialists in overseas biodiversity.
L. 134-3 LAW n°2016-1087 of August 8, 2016 - art. 14 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf When the National Biodiversity Committee and the National Nature Protection Council are seized of the same project, the two bodies each issue an opinion, which is made public. p.87 L. 141-1 LAW n°2012-1460 of December 27, 2012 - art. 15 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf When they have been carrying out their activities for at least three years, regularly declared associations carrying out their statutory activities in the field of nature protection and wildlife management, improvement of the living environment, the protection of water, air, soil, sites and landscapes, town planning, or having as its object the fight against pollution and nuisances and, in general, working mainly for the protection of the environment, may be the subject of a motivated approval of the administrative authority. The National Hunters' Federation, the regional hunters' federations, the interdepartmental hunters' federations and the departmental hunters' federations are eligible for the approval mentioned in the first paragraph. In the departments of Bas-Rhin, Haut-Rhin and Moselle, the approval procedure is applicable to associations registered for at least three years. These associations are called “approved environmental protection associations”. This approval is granted under conditions defined by decree of the Council of State. It is valid for a limited period and within a framework determined taking into account the ter territory in which the association actually carries out the activities set out in the first paragraph. It can be renewed. It can be repealed when the association no longer meets the conditions which led to its issue. Associations carrying out their activities in the fields mentioned in the first paragraph above and approved before February 3, 1995 are deemed approved pursuant to this article. Decisions taken pursuant to this article are subject to litigation of full jurisdiction. service-public.fr > Approval of environmental protection associations (AAPE): Approval of environmental protection associations t
L. 141-2 LAW n°2016-1087 of August 8, 2016 - art. 144 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf Environmental protection associations approved under article
L. 141-1 as well as the departmental federations of approved fishing and aquatic environment protection associations and approved associations of professional fishermen are called upon, within the framework of the laws and regulations in force, to participate in the action of public bodies regarding the environment. When an environmental protection association approved under article
L. 141-1 is dissolved, undeveloped land acquired half with public funds for the purposes of environmental protection is devolved by the administrative authority to a public establishment of the State or a local authority under the conditions provided for by a decree in the Council of State.

L. 142-1 LAW n°2016-1087 of August 8, 2016 - art. 144 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf Any association whose aim is to protect nature and the environment may initiate proceedings before the administrative courts for any grievance relating to this. Any environmental protection association approved under article
L. 141-1 as well as the departmental federations of approved fishing and aquatic environment protection associations and approved associations of professional fishermen demonstrate an interest in taking action against any administrative decision having a direct relationship with their statutory purpose and activities. and producing harmful effects for the environment in all or part of the territory for which they benefit from approval since this decision was made after the date of their approval. service-public.fr > Approval of environmental protection associations (AAPE): Legal action by associations
L. 142-2 LAW n°2016-1087 of August 8, 2016 - art. 101 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The approved associations mentioned in the article
L. 141-2 may exercise the rights recognized to the civil party with regard to facts causing direct or indirect harm to the collective interests which they aim to defend and constituting an infringement of the legislative provisions relating to the protection of nature and the environment, the improvement of the living environment, the protection of water, air, soil, sites and landscapes, town planning, maritime fishing or having the aim the fight against pollution and nuisances, nuclear safety and radiation protection, commercial practices and advertisements that are misleading or likely to mislead when these practices and advertisements include environmental information as well as the texts adopted for their application. This right is also recognized, under the same conditions, to associations regularly declared for at least five years at the date of the facts and which propose, through their statutes, the protection of all or part of the interests referred to in article
L. 211-1, with regard to the facts constituting an infringement of the provisions relating to water, or the interests referred to in article

L. 142-3 Ordinance 2000-914 2000-09-18 JORF September 21, 2000 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf When several identified natural persons have suffered individual harm which was caused by the act of the same person and which has a common origin, in the areas mentioned in article
L. 142-2, any association approved under article
L. 141-1 may, if it has been mandated by at least two of the natural persons concerned, seek compensation before any court on their behalf. The mandate cannot be requested. It must be given in writing by each natural person concerned. Any natural person who has given his or her consent to the taking of an action before a criminal court is considered in this case as exercising the rights recognized to the civil party, in application of the code of criminal procedure. However, notifications are sent to the association. The association which takes legal action in application of the provisions of the preceding paragraphs may become a civil party before the investigating judge or the trial court of the head office of the company in question or, failing that, of the place of the first offense. service-public.fr > Approval of environmental protection associations (AAPE): Approval of environmental protection associations
L. 142-3 -1 LAW n°2016-1547 of November 18, 2016 - art. 89 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I. - Subject to this article, Chapter I of Title V of Law No. 2016-1547 of November 18, 2016 on the modernization of justice for the 21st century and Chapter X of Title VII of Book VII of the Administrative Justice Code apply. apply to the action initiated on the basis of this article. II. - When several people placed in a similar situation suffer harm resulting from damage in the areas mentioned in the article
L. 142-2 of this code, caused by the same person, having as common cause a failure of the same nature to fulfill their legal or contractual obligations, a group action may be brought before a civil or administrative court. III. - This action may aim at stopping the breach, at repairing bodily and material damage resulting from the damage caused to the environment or for both purposes. IV. - Only the following may exercise this action: 1° Associations, approved under conditions defined by decree of the Council of State, whose statutory purpose includes the defense of victims of bodily injury or the defense of the economic interests of their members; 2° Environmental protection associations approved pursuant to article L. 141-1

Book I: Common provisions - Title VI: Prevention and repair of certain damage caused to the environment Title VI: Prevention and repair of certain damage caused to the environment t
L. 160-1 LAW n°2008-757 of August 1, 2008 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf This title defines the conditions under which damage caused to the environment by the activity of an operator is prevented or repaired, in application of the polluter pays principle and at a reasonable cost for society. The operator means any natural or legal person, public or private, who exercises or effectively controls, in a professional capacity, a lucrative or non-profitable economic activity. Chapter I: Scope of application
L. 161-1 LAW n°2016-1087 of August 8, 2016 - art. 149 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I. - Constitutes damage caused to the environment within the meaning of this title, direct or indirect measurable deterioration of the environment which: 1° Creates a risk of serious harm to human health due to soil contamination resulting from the direct or indirect introduction, on the surface or in the ground, of substances, mixtures, organisms or micro-organisms; 2° Seriously affect the ecological, chemical or quantitative state or the ecological potential of waters, including those of the exclusive economic zone, the territorial sea and French internal waters, with the exception of the cases provided for in VII of the article
L. 212-1; 3° Seriously affect the maintenance or reestablishment in a favorable conservation status: a) Of the species referred to in 2 of Article 4, in Annex I to Council Directive 79/409/EEC of April 2, 1979, concerning the conservation of wild birds and Annexes II and IV of Council Directive 92/43/EEC of 21 May 1992 on the conservation of natural habitats and wild fauna and flora; b) Habitats of the species referred to in 2 of Article 4, in Annex I to Council Directive 79/409/EEC of April 2, 1979, cited above and in Annex II to Directive 92/43/ EEC of the Council, of May 21, 1992, cited above as well as natural habitats listed in Annex I of the same Directive 92/43/EEC of the Council, of May 21, 1992; c) Breeding sites and resting areas of species listed in Annex IV of Council Directive 92/43/EEC of May 21, 1992, cited above; 4° Affect ecological services, that is to say the functions provided by soil, water and the species and habitats mentioned in 3° for the benefit of one of these natural resources or for the benefit of the public, exclusion of services rendered to the public by developments carried out by the operator or owner. II. - This title does not apply to damage or the imminent threat of damage referred to in 3° of I caused by: 1° The carrying out of programs or projects of activities, works, developments, works or installations as well as events and interventions in the natural environment or landscape as long as they have been authorized or approved under the conditions defined in article
L. 414-4; 2° An activity authorized or approved in application of articles L. 411-2 L. 411-5
L. 411-6, as long as the requirements arising from these articles have been respected. III. - Constitutes an imminent threat of damage caused to the environment for the application of this title a sufficient probability that such damage will occur in the near future. p.92 Environmental Code http://legifrance.gouv.fr/affichTexte.do?cidTexte=JORFTEXT000019277729 https://legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/article_lc/LEGIARTI000019280423/2024-05-14 https:// www.courdecassation.fr/recherche-judilibre?search_api_fulltext=%22L%20160-1%22+%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&judilibre_juridiction=cc&op=Rechercher+sur+judilibre https://www.courdecassation.fr/recherche -judilib re?search_api_fulltext=%22L%20160-1%22+%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&judilibre_juridiction=ca&op=Rechercher+sur+judilibre https://legifrance.gouv.fr/search/cetat?tab_selection=cetat&searchField=ALL&query =%22article%20L160-1%22%20du%20%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&page=1&init=true&dateDecision= https://juricaf.org/recherche/article%20L.%20160-1%20%22Code %20de%20l%27environnement%22/facet_pays%3AFrance?tri=DESC http://legifrance.gouv.fr/affichTexte.do?cidTexte=JORFTEXT000033016237 https://legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/article_lc/LEGIARTI000033035418/2024 -05-14 https://www.courdecassation.fr/recherche-judilibre?search_api_fulltext=%22L%20161-1%22+%22Code%20de%20l%27en vironnement%22&judilibre_juridiction=cc&op=Rechercher+sur+judilibre https://www.courdecassation.fr/recherche-judilibre?search_api_fulltext=%22L%20161-1%22+%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&judilibre_juridiction=ca&op=Search +sur+judilibre https://legifrance.gouv.fr/search/cetat?tab_selection=cetat&searchField=ALL&query=%22article%20L161-1%22%20du%20%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&page=1&init=true&dateDecision = https://juricaf.org/recherche/article%20L.%20161-1%20%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22/facet_pays%3AFrance?tri=DESC Legislative part - Book I: Common provisions - Title VI : Prevention and repair of certain damage caused to the environment t
L. 161-2 LAW n°2008-757 of August 1, 2008 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf This title does not apply to damage to the environment or the imminent threat of such damage: 1° Caused by armed conflict, civil war or insurrection; 2° Resulting from activities carried out mainly in the interest of national defense or international security other than those subject to declaration or authorization and provided for by articles L. 214-1
L. 214-10 and by title I of book V; 3° Caused by a natural phenomenon of an exceptional, inevitable and irresistible nature; 4° Resulting from activities whose sole purpose is protection against major natural risks or natural disasters; 5° Resulting from an event subject to a liability or compensation regime provided for by the international conventions mentioned in Annex IV of Directive 2004/35/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council, of April 21, 2004, on the environmental liability with regard to the prevention and repair of environmental damage, from their entry into force on the territory of the French Republic; 6° Resulting from activities covered by the Treaty establishing the European Atomic Energy Community, or from an incident or activity falling within the scope of the conventions referred to in Annex V to Directive 2004/35 /EC of the European Parliament and of the Council, of April 21, 2004, cited above; 7° Caused by pollution of a diffuse nature, unless a causal link between the damage or its threat and the activities of the different operators is established by the authority referred to in 2° of the article
L. 165-2, which may request the necessary assessments and information from the operator.
L. 161-3 LAW n°2008-757 of August 1, 2008 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf This title applies without prejudice to the right of a shipowner to limit his liability in application of the London Convention of November 19, 1976 on the limitation of liability for maritime claims and, from its entry into force in force in the territory of the French Republic, of the Strasbourg Convention of November 4, 1988 on the limitation of liability in inland navigation.
L. 161-4 LAW n°2008-757 of August 1, 2008 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf This title does not apply when more than thirty years have passed since the event causing the damage.
L. 161-5 LAW n°2008-757 of August 1, 2008 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf This title is also not applicable: 1° When the event giving rise to the damage occurred before April 30, 2007; 2° When the event giving rise to the damage results from an activity having https://legifrance.gouv.fr/search/cetat?tab_selection=cetat&searchField=ALL&query=%22article%20L161-5%22%20du%20%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&page=1&init=true&dateDecision= https ://juricaf.org/recherche/article%20L.%20161-5%20%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22/facet_pays%3AFrance?tri=DESC Legislative part - Book I: Common provisions - Title VI: Prevention and repair of certain damage caused to the environment Chapter II: Regime Section 1: Principles
L. 162-1 LAW n°2021-1104 of August 22, 2021 - art. 73 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The following are prevented or repaired according to the terms defined by this title: 1° Damage caused to the environment by professional activities, the list of which is established by the decree provided for in article
L. 165-2 or by activities governed by the mining code falling under the legal regime of mines or the legal regime of underground storage, including in the absence of fault or negligence of the operator; 2° Damage caused to the species and habitats referred to in 3° of I of the article
L. 161-1 by a professional activity other than those mentioned in 1° of this article, in the event of fault or negligence of the operator. The causal link between the activity and the damage is established by the authority referred to in 2° of the article
L. 165-2 which may request the necessary assessments and information from the operator.
L. 162-2 LAW n°2008-757 of August 1, 2008 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf A person who is the victim of harm resulting from environmental damage or an imminent threat of such damage cannot seek compensation on the basis of this title. Section 2: Measures to prevent or repair damage Subsection 1: Preventive measures
L. 162-3 LAW n°2008-757 of August 1, 2008 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf In the event of an imminent threat of damage, the operator takes preventive measures without delay and at its own expense in order to prevent the occurrence or limit its effects. If the threat persists, he immediately informs the authority referred to in 2° of article
L. 165-2 of its nature, the preventive measures it took and their results.
L. 162-4 LAW n°2008-757 of August 1, 2008 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf In the event of damage, the operator immediately informs the authority referred to in 2° of the article
L. 165-2. It takes measures without delay and at its own expense to put an end to its causes, to prevent or limit its worsening as well as its impact on human health and ecological services.
L. 162-5 Ordinance no. 2019-964 of September 18, 2019 - art. 35 (VD) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf To implement the preventive measures provided for in this subsection on private properties, the operator must first obtain written authorization from the owners, holders of real rights, their beneficiaries or, where applicable, holders of a right of use. He may conclude an agreement with them providing, where applicable, the terms of the authorization or the payment of compensation for occupation of land. p.94 Environmental Code Common provisions - Title VI: Prevention and repair of certain damage caused to the environment In the absence of amicable agreement or in cases of emergency, authorization may be given by the president of the judicial court or a magistrate designated by him . Subsection 2: Remedial measures not
L. 162-6 LAW n°2008-757 of August 1, 2008 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The authority referred to in 2° of the article
L. 165-2 evaluates the nature and consequences of the damage. It may ask the operator to carry out its own assessment.
L. 162-7 LAW n°2008-757 of August 1, 2008 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The operator submits for approval to the authority referred to in 2° of the article
L. 165-2 appropriate remedial measures with regard to the objectives defined in articles L. 162-8 L. 162-9
L. 162-8 LAW n°2008-757 of August 1, 2008 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf In the cases referred to in 1° of I of the article
L. 161-1, the repair measures must make it possible to eliminate any risk of serious harm to human health by taking into account the use of the damaged site existing or planned at the time of the damage, assessed in particular on the basis of the construction documents. town planning in force on that date. The possibility of soil repair by natural regeneration must be considered.
L. 162-9 LAW n°2008-757 of August 1, 2008 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf Measures to repair damage affecting the waters and the species and habitats mentioned in 2° and 3° of I of the article
L. 161-1 aim to restore these natural resources and their ecological services to their initial state and to eliminate any risk of serious harm to human health. The initial state designates the state of the natural resources and ecological services at the time of the damage, which would have existed if the environmental damage had not occurred, estimated using the best information available. Primary repair means any measure by which the natural resources and their services referred to in the first paragraph return to or approach their initial state. The possibility of repair by natural regeneration should be considered. When primary repair does not result in this return to the initial state or a state approaching it, additional repair measures must be implemented in order to provide a level of natural resources or services comparable to that which would have been provided if the site had been restored to its original state. They can be implemented on another site, the choice of which must take into account the interests of the populations affected by the damage. Compensatory reparation measures must compensate for intermediate losses of natural resources or services occurring between the damage and the date on which the primary or additional reparation produced its effect. They can be implemented on another site and cannot result in financial compensation.
L. 162-10 LAW n°2008-757 of August 1, 2008 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf After having, where applicable, asked the operator to complete or modify its proposals, the authority referred to in 2° of article
L. 165-2 submits them for opinion to local authorities or their groups, public establishments and environmental protection associations concerned because of their purpose, location, importance or nature of the damage . It also submits them to people likely to be affected by the reparation measures. It can make them available to the public. p.95 Environmental Code http://legifrance.gouv.fr/affichTexte.do?cidTexte=JORFTEXT000019277729 L. 162-11 LAW n°2008-757 of August 1, 2008 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf After having given the operator the opportunity to present its observations, the authority referred to in 2° of article
L. 165-2 prescribes to him, by a reasoned decision, the appropriate remedial measures.
L. 162-12 LAW n°2020-105 of February 10, 2020 - art. 94 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I.-The remedial measures prescribed by the authority referred to in 2° of the article
L. 165-2 are implemented on private properties under the conditions provided for in article
L. 162-5. II.-To facilitate this implementation, the authority referred to in 2° of article
L. 165-2 may, if the extent of the surface area or the number of land owners affected by these measures justifies it: 1° Apply, for the carrying out of the work, the law of December 29, 1892 on damage caused to property private through the execution of public works; 2° Establish public utility easements on land affected by the repair measures; these easements may include the limitation or prohibition of the use or modifications of the soil and subsoil; they are established and compensated under the conditions provided for by articles L. 515-9
L. 515-11; 3° Request that the repair work be declared of public utility and, where applicable, the acquisition for the benefit of a public entity of the buildings affected by the damage. Section 3: Administrative police powers
L. 162-13 Ordinance No. 2012-34 of January 11, 2012 - art. 2 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf In the event of an imminent threat of damage, or when such damage has occurred, the authority referred to in 2° of article
L. 165-2 may at any time ask the operator required to prevent or repair damage under this title to provide him with all useful information relating to this threat or damage and to the prevention or repair measures. provided for by this title.
L. 162-14 Ordinance No. 2012-34 of January 11, 2012 - art. 2 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf When the operator has not taken the measures provided for in articles L. 162-3
L. 162-4 or that he has not implemented the remedial measures prescribed pursuant to article
L. 162-11, the authority mentioned in 2° of the article
L. 165-2 implements the provisions provided for in article L. 171-8
L. 162-15 LAW n°2008-757 of August 1, 2008 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf In the event of an emergency and when the operator required to prevent or repair damage under this title cannot be immediately identified, local authorities or their groups, public establishments, public interest groups, associations protection of the environment, professional unions, foundations, owners of property affected by the damage or their associations may propose to the authority referred to in 2° of the article
L. 165-2 to carry out preventive or remedial measures themselves in accordance with the objectives defined in articles L. 162-3 L. 162-4 L. 162-8
L. 162-9. The procedures provided for in articles L. 162-5 L. 162-11 L. 162-14
L. 162-16 are applicable. p.96 Environmental Code http://legifrance.gouv.fr/affichTexte.do?cidTexte=JORFTEXT000019277729 https://legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/article_lc/LEGIARTI000019280347/2024-05-14 L. 162-16 LAW n°2008-757 of August 1, 2008 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The authority referred to in 2° of the article
L. 165-2 may, at any time, in the event of an emergency or serious danger, take the necessary preventive or remedial measures itself or have them taken, at the expense of the defaulting operator. Section 4: Cost of prevention and repair measures
L. 162-17 LAW n°2008-757 of August 1, 2008 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The operator required to prevent or repair damage in application of this title bears the costs related to: 1° The assessment of the damage; 2° The determination, implementation and monitoring of prevention and remedial measures; 3° If applicable, to the consultation procedures provided for in the first two sentences of the article
L. 162-10; 4° Where applicable, compensation paid pursuant to articles L. 162-5 L. 162-12
L. 162-18 LAW n°2008-757 of August 1, 2008 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf When environmental damage has several causes, the cost of prevention or repair measures is distributed by the authority referred to in 2° of the article
L. 165-2 between operators, up to the extent of the contribution of their activity to the damage or the imminent threat of damage.
L. 162-19 LAW n°2008-757 of August 1, 2008 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf When it has carried out or has carried out the ex officio execution of preventive or reparative measures without recourse to the provisions of 1° of II of article
L. 162-14, the authority referred to in 2° of article
L. 165-2 recovers the cost from the operator whose activity caused the damage. It may decide not to recover the costs incurred when the amount of expenses necessary for this recovery is greater than the sum to be recovered.
L. 162-20 LAW n°2008-757 of August 1, 2008 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The persons referred to in the article
L. 162-15 are entitled to reimbursement by the operator required to prevent or repair the damage under this title, when it has been identified, of the costs they incurred for the implementation of the repair measures or prevention, without prejudice to compensation for other damages suffered. The request is addressed to the authority referred to in 2° of article
L. 165-2 which, after collecting the operator's observations, sets the amount that the latter must reimburse.
L. 162-21 LAW n°2008-757 of August 1, 2008 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The authority referred to in 2° of the article
L. 165-2 may initiate cost recovery proceedings against the operator within a period of five years from the date on which the prescribed measures were completed or from the date on which the responsible operator was identified, the most recent date being retained.
L. 162-22 LAW n°2008-757 of August 1, 2008 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The operator may recover by any appropriate legal means, from the persons responsible, the cost of the preventive or reparative measures which he has undertaken in application of this title, when he can prove that the damage or its imminent threat : 1° Is the act of a third party, despite appropriate security measures; p.97 Environmental Code L. 162-23 LAW n°2008-757 of August 1, 2008 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The cost of the measures referred to in articles L. 162-4 L. 162-8
L. 162-9 cannot be made the responsibility of the operator if he provides proof that he has not committed fault or negligence and that the damage to the environment results from an emission, 'an activity or, within the framework of an activity, any mode of use of a product which was not considered likely to cause damage to the environment in view of the state of scientific and technical knowledge at the time of the event giving rise to the damage. Chapter III: Restoration of biodiversity, renaturation and compensation for damage to biodiversity Section 1: Natural sites for compensation, restoration and renaturation
L. 163-1 -A. LAW n°2023-973 of October 23, 2023 - art. 15 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I. - Restoration or development operations for elements of biodiversity can be implemented by public or private persons on sites called “natural compensation, restoration and renaturation sites”. The ecological gain from the operations mentioned in the first paragraph of this I is identified by compensation, restoration or renaturation units. These units may be sold by the persons mentioned in the same first paragraph to any other public or private person. Natural compensation, restoration and renaturation sites are subject to prior approval from the competent administrative authority. The issuance of approval takes into account in particular the expected ecological gain, the integration of the site into the ecological continuities mentioned in Title VII of Book III, its surface area and the anthropogenic pressures exerted on this site. II. - Any person subject to an obligation to implement measures to compensate for attacks on biodiversity defined in I of the article
L. 163-1 may satisfy this in advance by the use or acquisition of compensation, restoration or renaturation units, in compliance with the principles defined in the same I. III. - A decree specifies the terms of application of this article, in particular the terms of approval and monitoring of natural compensation, restoration and renaturation sites as well as the nature and terms of sale of compensation, restoration or renaturation. IV. - Natural compensation, restoration and renaturation sites may give rise to the allocation of carbon credits under the “low-carbon” label, subject to respecting the principles set out in article
L. 229-55 and the method of this label approved by the Minister responsible for the environment. p.98 Environmental Code http://legifrance.gouv.fr/affichTexte.do?cidTexte=JORFTEXT000019277729 https://legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/article_lc/LEGIARTI000019280368/2024-05-14 https:// www.courdecassation.fr/recherche-judilibre?search_api_fulltext=%22L%20162-23%22+%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&judilibre_juridiction=cc&op=Rechercher+sur+judilibre https://www.courdecassation.fr/recherche -judilibre?search_api_fulltext=%22L%20162-23%22+%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&judilibre_juridiction=ca&op=Rechercher+sur+judilibre https://legifrance.gouv.fr/search/cetat?tab_selection=cetat&searchField= ALL&query=%22article%20L162-23%22%20du%20%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&page=1&init=true&dateDecision= https://juricaf.org/recherche/article%20L.%20162-23%20% 22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22/facet_pays%3AFrance?tri=DESC http://legifrance.gouv.fr/affichTexte.do?cidTexte=JORFTEXT000048242288 https://legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/article_lc/LEGIARTI000048247891/ 2024-05-14 https://www.courdecassation.fr/recherche-judilibre?search_api_fulltext=%22L%20163-1-A%20%22+%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&judilibre_juridiction=cc&op=Rechercher+sur +judilibre https://www.courdecassation.fr/recherche-judilibre?search_api_fulltext=%22L%20163-1-A%20%22+%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&judilibre_juridiction=ca&op=Rechercher+sur+judilibre https ://legifrance.gouv.fr/search/cetat?tab_selection=cetat&searchField=ALL&query=%22article%20L163-1-A%22%20du%20%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&page=1&init=true&dateDecision= https: //juricaf.org/recherche/article%20L.%20163-1-A.%20%20%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22/facet_pays%3AFrance?tri=DESC Legislative part - Book I: Common provisions - Title VI: Prevention and repair of certain damage caused to the environment Section 2: Compensation for damage to biodiversity
L. 163-1 LAW n°2023-973 of October 23, 2023 - art. 15 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I. - The measures to compensate for damage to biodiversity are the measures provided for in 2° of II of the article
L. 110-1 and made obligatory by a legislative or regulatory text to compensate, while respecting their ecological equivalence, the planned or foreseeable damage to biodiversity caused by the execution of a works or structure project or by the carrying out activities or executing a plan, scheme, program or other planning document. Measures to compensate for damage to biodiversity aim to achieve the objective of no net loss, or even gain, of biodiversity. They must result in an obligation to achieve results and be effective throughout the duration of the infringement. They cannot replace avoidance and reduction measures. If the harm linked to the project cannot be avoided, reduced or compensated satisfactorily, it is not authorized as it stands. II. - Any person subject to an obligation to implement measures to compensate for damage to biodiversity satisfies them either directly, or by entrusting, by contract, the implementation of these measures to a compensation operator defined in III of this article, or by acquiring compensation, restoration or renaturation units within the framework of a natural compensation, restoration and renaturation site defined in article
L. 163-1 A. When the compensation relates to a project, plan or program subject to environmental assessment, the nature of the compensation proposed by the project owner is specified in the impact study presented by the petitioner with his request for authorization. In all cases, the project owner remains solely responsible towards the administrative authority which prescribed these compensation measures. The compensation arrangements mentioned in the first paragraph of this II may be implemented alternatively or cumulatively. Compensation measures are implemented as a priority on the damaged site or, in any case, in functional proximity to it in order to guarantee its functionality in the long term. The same measurement can compensate for different functionalities. The compensation measures are implemented as a priority within the preferential renaturation zones identified by the territorial coherence schemes in application of 3° of the article
L. 141-10 of the town planning code and by the development and programming guidelines relating to sectors to be renatured in application of 4° of I of the article
L. 151-7 of the same code, when the renaturation guidelines of these zones or sectors and the nature of the compensation planned for the project allow it. A decree of the Council of State specifies the terms of application of this paragraph. III. - A compensation operator is a public or private person charged, by a person subject to an obligation to implement measures to compensate for damage to biodiversity, to implement them on behalf of this person and to coordinate them at long term.
L. 163-2 LAW n°2023-973 of October 23, 2023 - art. 15 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf When measures to compensate for damage to biodiversity are implemented on land belonging neither to the person subject to the obligation to implement these measures, nor to the compensation operator that they have designated, a contract concluded with the owner and, where applicable, the tenant or operator defines the nature of the compensation measures and their implementation methods, as well as their duration.
L. 163-4 LAW n°2023-973 of October 23, 2023 - art. 15 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf When a person subject to an obligation to implement measures to compensate for damage to biodiversity has not complied with the conditions imposed on him, the administrative authority p.99 https://legifrance.gouv.fr/search/cetat?tab_selection=cetat&searchField=ALL&query=%22article%20L163-4%22%20du%20%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement %22&page=1&init=true&dateDecision= https://juricaf.org/recherche/article%20L.%20163-4%20%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22/facet_pays%3AFrance?tri=DESC Legislative part - Book I : Common provisions - Title VI: Prevention and repair of certain damage caused to the environment competent authority gives it formal notice to comply within a period that it determines, under the conditions provided for in Article THE
L. 171-8. When, at the end of the time limit, the person has not complied with this formal notice and the measures taken pursuant to II of Article
L. 171-8 have not made it possible to regularize the situation, the competent administrative authority shall proceed ex officio, in place of this person and at the latter's expense, to the execution of the prescribed measures, in entrusting the carrying out of these measures to a compensation operator or by acquiring compensation, restoration or renaturation units within the framework of a natural compensation, restoration and renaturation site, under the conditions provided for in II of the article
L. 163-1, which correspond to the characteristics of the prescribed measures. When it finds that the measures to compensate for damage to biodiversity are ineffective in respecting ecological equivalence according to the terms and conditions which have been established by regulation, the competent administrative authority orders additional requirements. Any person subject to an obligation to implement measures to compensate for damage to biodiversity may be required by the competent administrative authority to provide financial guarantees. These guarantees are intended to ensure the completion of the compensation measures provided for in this chapter. Without prejudice to the administrative fine procedure provided for in 4° of II of Article
L. 171-8, breaches of financial guarantee obligations give rise to the application of the deposit procedure provided for in 1° of the same II, independently of the criminal proceedings which may be brought.
L. 163-5 LAW n°2023-973 of October 23, 2023 - art. 15 (V) Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf Measures to compensate for damage to biodiversity defined in I of the article
L. 163-1 are geolocated and described in a national geographic information system, accessible to the public on the internet. The project owners provide the competent State services with all the information necessary for the proper maintenance of this tool by these services. Chapter IV: Special provisions for certain activities
L. 164-1 LAW n°2008-757 of August 1, 2008 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The application of this title does not prevent the implementation of any special police regime.
L. 164-2 LAW n°2016-1087 of August 8, 2016 - art. 4 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The reparation measures taken pursuant to this title take into account those taken, where applicable, pursuant to Chapter III of Subtitle II of Title III of Book III of the Civil Code.
