L. 211-11 LAW n°2016-41 of January 26, 2016 - art. 56 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The specific provisions relating to the quality of water intended for human consumption are set out in the Public Health Code (first part, book III, title II, chapters I, II and IV). Those relating to bathing water are set out in the same code (first part, book III, title III, chapter II).
L. 211-11 -1 Ordinance No. 2022-1611 of December 22, 2022 - art. 3 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf When the results of analyzes of the quality of water coming directly from a sampling point, used for the production of water intended for human consumption, show, for the parameters defined by order of the ministers responsible for environment and health, levels exceeding the thresholds set by this same decree taking into account the requirements mentioned in the second paragraph of I of the article
L. 1321-1 of the Public Health Code, the sampling point is considered sensitive.
L. 211-12 ORDER no. 2014-1345 of November 6, 2014 - art. 5 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I. – Public utility easements may be established at the request of the State, local authorities or their groups on land bordering a watercourse or the diversion of a watercourse, or located in their watershed, or in an estuarine zone. II. – These easements may have one or more of the following purposes: 1° Create temporary retention zones for flood water or runoff, through developments making it possible to artificially increase their storage capacity for this water, in order to reduce flooding or runoff in areas located downstream; 2° Create or restore mobility zones of the minor bed of a watercourse upstream of urbanized areas in areas called "watercourse mobility zones", in order to preserve or restore its hydrological characteristics and essential geomorphological; 3° Preserve or restore wetlands known as “strategic areas for water management” demarcated pursuant to the article
L. 212-5 -1. III. – The areas subject to the easements referred to in 1° and 2° of II are delimited by prefectural decree. This is taken after a public inquiry carried out in accordance with Chapter III of Title II of Book I of this code. The areas subject to the easements referred to in 3° of II are delimited in accordance with article
L. 212-5 -1. IV. – In the temporary retention zones for flood water or runoff mentioned in 1° of II, the prefectural decree may require owners and operators to refrain from any act likely to harm the proper functioning, maintenance and conservation of structures intended to allow flooding of the area. To this end, the prefectural decree may require prior declaration, to the competent authorities in matters of town planning, works which, due to their nature, their importance or their location, are likely to obstruct the storage or to the flow of water and do not fall within the scope of the authorizations or declarations established by the town planning code. The prefectural decree may also require prior declaration of works which, due to their nature, their importance or their location, are likely to obstruct the storage or flow of water and do not fall within the scope. application of authorizations or declarations established by the town planning code. The prefect may, by reasoned decision, within two months of receipt of the declaration, oppose the carrying out of these works or prescribe the necessary work. Work on these works cannot begin before this deadline has expired. For the works referred to in the first paragraph of this IV, as well as for the works and structures subject to authorization or a declaration established by the town planning code and which are likely, because of their nature, their importance or their location, to obstruct the storage or flow of water, the authority competent to rule on town planning matters obtains the agreement of the prefect who has a period of two months from receipt of the declaration or the request for authorization to oppose the execution of the work or prescribe the necessary modifications. Work cannot begin before this deadline has expired. p.139 Environmental Code http://legifrance.gouv.fr/affichTexte.do?cidTexte=JORFTEXT000031912641 V. – In the mobility zones of a watercourse mentioned in 2° of II, bank protection work, embankments, embankments and scouring, constructions or installations and, in general, cannot be carried out. , all works or structures likely to obstruct the natural movement of the watercourse. To this end, the prefectural decree may require prior declaration, to the competent authorities in matters of town planning, works which, due to their nature, their importance or their location, are likely to obstruct natural movement. of the watercourse and do not fall within the scope of the authorizations or declarations established by the town planning code. The prefectural decree may also require prior declaration of works which, due to their nature, their importance or their location, are likely to obstruct the natural movement of the watercourse and do not fall within the scope of the declaration. application of authorizations or declarations established by the town planning code. The prefect may, by reasoned decision, within two months of receipt of the declaration, oppose the carrying out of these works or prescribe the necessary work. Work on these works cannot begin before this deadline has expired. For the work referred to in the first paragraph of this V, as well as for the work and works subject to authorization or a declaration established by the town planning code and which are likely, because of their nature, their importance or their location, to obstruct the natural movement of the watercourse, the competent authority to rule on matters of town planning obtains the agreement of the prefect who has a period of two months from receipt of the declaration or request for authorization to oppose the execution of the work or prescribe the necessary modifications. Work cannot begin before this deadline has expired. V bis. – In wetlands known as “strategic zones for water management” mentioned in 3° of II, the prefect may by order require owners and operators to refrain from any act likely to harm nature and to the role as well as the maintenance and conservation of the area, in particular drainage, backfilling and turning of meadow. VI. – The prefectural decree may identify, where applicable, the existing or missing elements obstructing the purpose of the easement, the removal, modification or establishment of which is made obligatory. The financial burden of the work and compensation for any damage that may result from it falls to the community which requested the establishment of the easement. However, if the said elements belong to the State or its public establishments, the responsibility for the work falls to the latter. VII. – When one of the objects for which the easement has been established involves the realization by the public community of installations, works or activities, the owners and operators are required to allow at all times the agents responsible for their development, maintenance or exploitation, to access land included in the perimeter of areas subject to easement. VIII. – The establishment of the easements mentioned in I gives right to compensation for owners of land in encumbered areas when they create material, direct and certain damage. These compensations are the responsibility of the community which requested the institution of the servitude. They are set, in the absence of an amicable agreement, by the expropriation judge competent in the department. IX. – Material damage affecting crops, crops, dead or alive livestock, motorized land vehicles and buildings caused by overflooding linked to temporary retention of water in the areas subject to easements mentioned in II gives rise to compensation for the occupants. However, natural or legal persons who have contributed through their actions or through their negligence to the occurrence of the damage are excluded from benefiting from compensation in the proportion to which the said damage can be attributable to them. These compensations are the responsibility of the community which requested the establishment of the easement encumbering the area. Damage affecting harvests, crops, buildings and dead or alive livestock affected by agricultural operations is assessed within the framework of local protocols of agreement. Failing this, they are evaluated under the conditions provided for in application of article e
L. 361-5 of the rural and maritime fishing code. x. – For a period of ten years from the date of publication of the prefectural decree noting the completion of the work mentioned in VI or, if such work is not necessary, from the date of publication of the decree prefectural institution establishing one or more of the easements mentioned in I, the owner p.140 Environmental Code https://legifrance.gouv.fr/affichCodeArticle.do?cidTexte=LEGITEXT000006071367&idArticle=LEGIARTI000006583626&dateTexte=&categorieLien=cid Legislative part - Book II: Physical environments - Title I: Water and aquatic and marine environments of a plot of land encumbered by one of these easements may require partial or total acquisition by the community which requested the institution of the easement. This right of abandonment is exercised under the conditions provided for in articles
L. 230-1 et seq. of the town planning code. The owner may, at the same time, require the partial or total acquisition of other plots of land if the existence of the easement compromises their exploitation or use under conditions similar to those existing before the institution of the easement. XI. – In the areas mentioned in II, the municipalities or competent public intermunicipal cooperation establishments may establish the right of urban pre-emption under the conditions defined in article
L. 211-1 of the town planning code. They can delegate this right to the community which requested the institution of the servitude. XII. – A decree of the Council of State establishes the conditions of application of this article.
L. 211-13 Ordinance No. 2010-462 of May 6, 2010 - art. 1 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I.-Notwithstanding any provisions to the contrary, the State, local authorities or their groups, having acquired land located in the temporary retention zones of flood water or runoff or the mobility zones of a watercourse referred to in article
L. 211-12 of this code may, during the establishment or renewal of rural leases referred to in Title I of Book IV of the Rural and Maritime Fishing Code relating to these lands, prescribe to the lessee modes of use of the ground in order to prevent flooding or aggravate potential damage. I bis.-Notwithstanding any provisions to the contrary, the State, local authorities or their groups, having acquired land located in the strategic areas for water management mentioned in article
L. 211-12 may, when establishing or renewing rural leases referred to in Title I of Book IV of the Rural and Maritime Fishing Code relating to these lands, prescribe to the lessee methods of use of the land in order to preserve or restore its nature and role. II.-By way of derogation from Title I of Book IV of the Rural and Maritime Fisheries Code, the administrative court has sole jurisdiction to settle disputes concerning leases established or renewed in application of I and I bis.
L. 211-14 Ordinance No. 2016-1060 of August 3, 2016 - art. 4 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I. - Along certain watercourses, sections of watercourses and bodies of water of more than ten hectares, the operator or, failing that, the occupant or owner of the riparian plot is required to implement place and maintain a permanent plant cover composed of species adapted to the surrounding natural ecosystem on the ground with a width of at least five meters from the bank, excluding spaces already waterproofed or occupied by buildings, courtyards , land enclosed by walls, without prejudice to the town planning rules applicable to said spaces. II. - The list of watercourses, sections of watercourses and bodies of water along which this obligation applies is drawn up by the administrative authority in accordance with the designation of watercourses under the protection regimes. direct support for farmers within the framework of the common agricultural policy, having regard to the objective of good ecological and chemical status of waters. The administrative authority may set conditions for managing the surface area under environmental cover, in particular in order to avoid the proliferation of weeds. The use of fertilizers and plant protection products is however prohibited, except stification of their safety for the environment or in the cases provided for by local rules of minimum maintenance, as well as the storage of products or waste. III. - The measures taken in application of this article give rise to the right to compensation for the occupants or owners of land in the areas concerned when they cause material damage, including direct and certain loss of income. These compensations are the responsibility of the State. They are set, in the absence of an amicable agreement, according to the procedure applicable before the expropriation judge. p.141 Environmental Code https://juricaf.org/recherche/article%20L.%20211-14%20%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22/facet_pays%3AFrance? tri=DESC Legislative part - Book II: Physical environments - Title I: Water and aquatic and marine environments Chapter II: Planning Section 1: Master plans for water development and management
L. 212-1 Ordinance No. 2022-1611 of December 22, 2022 - art. 3 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I. # The administrative authority delimits the basins or groups of basins by determining, where applicable, the bodies of groundwater and the internal and territorial maritime waters attached to them. II. # The competent basin committee carries out in each basin or group of basins: 1° An analysis of its characteristics and the impact of activities on the state of the water as well as an economic analysis of water uses; these analyzes are re-examined periodically; 2° The establishment and regular updating of one or more registers listing: # the zones being the subject of specific legislative or regulatory provisions in application of specific Community legislation relating to the protection of surface water or groundwater or the conservation of habitats or species directly dependent on water; # water bodies likely to be intended for current and future human consumption. 3° The identification, no later than December 31, 2027, of groundwater bodies and aquifers which include strategic resources for current or future drinking water supply as well as, if the information is available, their safeguarding zones, within which protection measures are instituted for the preservation of this s strategic resources. These measures help to ensure the quantitative balance between the withdrawals from these resources, taking into account in particular the needs of human activities and their capacity to replenish themselves naturally, and also contribute to preserving their quality to satisfy as a priority the needs for consumption. human. They also take into account the needs linked in particular to food production. III. # Each basin or group of hydrographic basins has one or more water development and management master plans setting out the objectives referred to in IV of this article and the guidelines enabling it to meet the principles provided for in Articles L. 211- 1
L. 430-1. The diagram takes into account the evaluation, by geographical area, of the hydroelectric potential established in application of I of Article 6 of Law No. 2000-108 of February 10, 2000 relating to the modernization and development of the public service of the 'electricity. IV. # The water quality and quantity objectives set out in the water development and management master plans correspond to: 1° For surface waters, with the exception of artificial water bodies or those heavily modified by human activities , in good ecological and chemical status; 2° For artificial surface water bodies or those heavily modified by human activities, with good ecological potential and good chemical status; 3° For groundwater bodies, to a good chemical state and to a balance between withdrawals and the renewal capacity of each of them; 4° To prevent the deterioration of water quality; 5° The specific requirements defined for the areas referred to in 2° of II, in particular in order to reduce the treatment necessary for the production of water intended for human consumption. V. # The objectives mentioned in IV must be achieved no later than December 22, 2015  However, if it appears that, for technical, financial reasons or those relating to natural conditions, the objectives mentioned in 1°, 2° and 3° of IV cannot be achieved before this date, the master development and construction plan water management can set longer deadlines, providing reasons for them, provided that the state of the body of water concerned does not deteriorate further. The postponements thus made cannot exceed the period corresponding to two updates of the water development and management master plan, except in cases where the natural conditions are such that the objectives cannot be achieved within this period. p.142 Environmental Code http://legifrance.gouv.fr/affichTexte.do?cidTexte=JORFTEXT000046780481 https://legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/article_lc/LEGIARTI000046783860/2024-05-14 https:// www.courdecassation.fr/recherche-judilibre?search_api_fulltext=%22L%20212-1%22+%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&judilibre_juridiction=cc&op=Rechercher+sur+judilibre https://www.courdecassation.fr/recherche -judilibre?search_api_fulltext=%22L%20212-1%22+%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&judilibre_juridiction=ca&op=Rechercher+sur+judilibre https://legifrance.gouv.fr/search/cetat? tab_selection=cetat&searchField=ALL&query=%22article%20L212-1%22%20du%20%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&page=1&init=true&dateDecision= https://juricaf.org/recherche/article%20L.%20212- 1%20%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22/facet_pays%3AFrance?tri=DESC Legislative part - Book II: Physical environments - Title I: Water and aquatic and marine environments VI. # When the achievement of the objectives mentioned in 1°, 2° and 3° of IV is impossible or involves a cost disproportionate to the benefits that can be expected, derogatory objectives may be set by the master plan of water development and management by motivating them. VII. # Changes in the physical characteristics of water or the exercise of new human activities may justify, under conditions defined by the decree provided for in XIII, justified exemptions from compliance with the objectives mentioned in 1° to 4° of IV and VI . The administrative authority draws up the list of these exemptions after having made it available to the public, in particular by electronic means, for a minimum period of six months in order to collect its observations. By way of derogation from the second paragraph of this VII, when, pursuant to article THE
L. 181-2, the environmental authorization takes the place of the exemption mentioned in the first paragraph of this VII, the public consultation provided for in I of article
L. 181-10 exempts, for the project concerned, from making the list of exemptions available to the public. VIII. # The water development and management master plan indicates how the costs linked to the use of water are borne by users, distinguishing at least the industrial sector, the agricultural sector and domestic uses. This data is updated when the master plan is updated. IX. # The master plan determines the necessary developments and provisions, including the implementation of the blue network appearing in the adopted regional ecological coherence plans mentioned in article
L. 371-3 or the regional plans for planning, sustainable development and equality of territories mentioned in article
L. 4251-1 of the general code of local authorities, to prevent deterioration and ensure the protection and improvement of the state of water and aquatic environments, to achieve and respect the objectives of quality and quantity of water mentioned in IV to VII. In particular, the master plan identifies the sub-basins or parts of sub-basins in which coordinated management of works, particularly hydroelectric works, is necessary. The water development and management master plan is compatible or made compatible, during its periodic update provided for in IV of the article
L. 212-2, with the environmental objectives defined by the action plan for the marine environment provided for in articles L. 219-9
L. 219-18. x. # The water development and management master plan determines the internal and territorial maritime waters and the sub-basins or groups of sub-basins for which a water development and management plan defined in article
L. 212-3 is necessary to respect the fundamental orientations and objectives set pursuant to this article, and sets the deadline within which the water development and management plan must be developed and revised. Failing this, the administrative authority sets the scope and the deadline according to the terms provided for in article
L. 212-3. XI. # Programs and administrative decisions in the field of water must be compatible or made compatible with the provisions of the water development and management master plans. XII. # In the case of basins or groups of basins extending beyond the border, their delimitation provided for in I, the objectives mentioned in IV as well as the developments and provisions referred to in IX are defined in coordination with the competent foreign authorities. XIII. # A decree of the Council of State sets out the terms of application of this article.
L. 212-2 Ordinance No. 2016-1060 of August 3, 2016 - art. 4 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I. # The relevant basin committee in each basin or group of basins develops and updates the water development and management master plan(s) and monitors their application. II. # The basin committee organizes public participation in the development of the water development and management master plan. It develops and makes available to the public, for a minimum period of six months electronically in order to collect its observations: # the timetable and the work program indicating the methods of developing or updating the master plan, three is at least years before the planned date of entry into force of the water development and management master plan; # a provisional summary of the important questions that arise in the basin or group of basins in terms of water management, at least two years before the planned date of entry into force of the master water development and management plan ; p.143 Environmental Code http://legifrance.gouv.fr/affichTexte.do?cidTexte=JORFTEXT000032966914 https://legifrance.gouv.fr/codes/article_lc/LEGIARTI000032973269/2024-05-14 https:// www.courdecassation.fr/recherche-judilibre?search_api_fulltext=%22L%20212-2%22+%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&judilibre_juridiction=cc&op=Rechercher+sur+judil ibre https://www.courdecassation.fr/recherche-judilibre?search_api_fulltext=%22L%20212-2%22+%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&judilibre_juridiction=ca&op=Rechercher+sur+judilibre https://legifrance. gouv.fr/search/cetat?tab_selection=cetat&searchField=ALL&query=%22article%20L212-2%22%20du%20%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22&page=1&init=true&dateDecision= https://juricaf.org/recherche /article%20L.%20212-2%20%22Code%20de%20l%27environnement%22/facet_pays%3AFrance?tri=DESC Legislative part - Book II: Physical environments - Title I: Water and aquatic and marine environments # the project master plan for water development and management as well as the environmental assessment required pursuant to article e
L. 122-4 of this code, at least one year before the planned date of its entry into force. This provision is made electronically. A computer station is made available free of charge to the public in a specific location in order to consult an electronic version of the file. A copy of the file can be consulted on paper at a specific location from the time it is made available. The terms of these consultations are made known to the public at least fifteen days before the start of making these documents available electronically and through local publication. The basin committee may modify the draft plan to take into account the opinions and observations made. The basin committee publishes at the end of each phase of public participation and at the latest on the date of adoption of the master plan, a summary of the opinions and observations collected and the way in which it took them into account. III. # The water development and management master plan is adopted by the basin committee and approved by the administrative authority. It is made available to the public. IV. # It is updated every six years. V. # It can be adapted under the conditions defined in article
L. 300-6 -1 of the town planning code. VI. # A decree of the Council of State specifies the terms of application of this article. It determines the conditions under which the administrative authority replaces the basin committee if it appears that the missions entrusted to it cannot be fulfilled within the deadlines as well as the procedure followed for this purpose.
L. 212-2 -1 LAW n°2012-387 of March 22, 2012 - art. 67 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The administrative authority establishes and periodically updates for each basin or group of basins a multi-annual program of measures contributing to the achievement of the objectives and provisions of the water development and management master plan. It organizes public participation in the development of the multi-annual program of measures according to the terms provided for in Article II
L. 212-2. This program as well as its periodic update are subject to the opinion of the basin committee.
L. 212-2 -2 LAW n°2016-1087 of August 8, 2016 - art. 117 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The administrative authority establishes and updates for each basin or group of basins, after consulting the basin committee, a water state monitoring program. Riparian owners of non-state watercourses, lakes and bodies of water are required to allow free passage over their land to agents mandated by the administrative authority to access said watercourses, lakes and bodies of water and carry out the measures necessary for the implementation and monitoring of the water state monitoring program, to the extent necessary to accomplish this mission. The analyzes of water, biota and sediments necessary for the implementation of the monitoring program are carried out by laboratories approved for environmental protection. Circulars and Instructions > Technical note of September 4, 2018 relating to the interim reports of the measurement programs and the 2018 European reporting.
L. 212-2 -3 Law n°2004-338 of April 21, 2004 - art. 4 () JORF April 22, 2004 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf In the case of basins or groups of basins extending beyond the border, the administrative authority develops the programs provided for in articles L. 212-2
L. 212-2 -2, in coordination with the competent foreign authorities. p.144 Environmental Code https://juricaf.org/recherche/article%20L.%20212-2-3%20%22Code %20de%20l%27environnement%22/facet_pays%3AFrance?tri=DESC Legislative part - Book II: Physical environments - Title I: Water and aquatic L. 212-3 Law n°2006-1772 of December 30, 2006 - art. 75 () JORF December 31, 2006 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The water development and management plan established for a sub-basin, for a group of sub-basins corresponding to a coherent hydrographic unit or for an aquifer system establishes the general objectives and the provisions making it possible to satisfy the principles set out in the articles L. 211-1
L. 430-1. The water development and management plan must be compatible with the water development and management master plan provided for in article
L. 212-1 or made compatible with it within three years following the update of the master plan. The scope and time frame within which it is developed or revised are determined by the water development and management master plan; failing this, they are decided by the representative of the State in the department, on proposal or after consultation of the local authorities and after consultation of the public territorial basin establishments and the basin committee. In the latter case, the State representative in the department may complete the local water commission while respecting the distribution of seats provided for in II of article L. 212-4
L. 212-4 Ordinance No. 2016-1060 of August 3, 2016 - art. 4 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I.-For the development, modification, revision and monitoring of the application of the water development and management plan, a local water commission is created by the prefect. The implementation of the water development and management plan is ensured by a public territorial basin establishment when it results from the recognition procedure resulting from the decree of February 7, 2005 relating to the delimitation of the perimeter of intervention of the public territorial basin establishment or when the perimeter of the water development and management plan implemented by this public territorial basin establishment has been delimited after the adoption of law no. 2010-788 of 12 July 2010 establishing a national commitment for the environment and provided that the scope of this water development and management plan is not included in the scope of a group of local authorities but is included in that of the public establishment basin territory. II.-The local water commission includes: 1° Representatives of local authorities and their groups, local public establishments and, if they exist, the territorial public basin establishment, located in whole or in part in the perimeter of the diagram referred to in article
L. 212-3, who designate the president of the commission from among themselves; 2° Representatives of users, landowners, professional organizations and associations concerned, established within the scope of the plan referred to in article
L. 212-3; 3° Representatives of the State and its interested public establishments. The representatives of the category mentioned in 1° hold at least half of the total number of seats and those of the category mentioned in 2° at least a quarter. A decree sets the rules for designating representatives of the different categories.
L. 212-5 Law n°2006-1772 of December 30, 2006 - art. 77 () JORF December 31, 2006 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf The water development and management plan provides an overview of the state of water resources and the aquatic environment. It lists the different uses that are made of existing water resources. It takes into account the orientation documents and programs of the State, local authorities and their groups, mixed unions, public establishments, other legal entities under public law, as well as mixed economy companies and free trade union associations of Ordinance No. 2004-632 of July 1, 2004 relating to trade union associations of owners having an impact on the quality, distribution or use of water resources. L. 212-5 -1 LAW no. 2021-1104 of August 22, 2021 - art. 61 Legif. Plan Jp.C.Cass. Jp.Call Jp.Admin. Juricaf I. — The water development and management plan includes a sustainable development and management plan for water resources and aquatic environments defining the conditions for achieving the objectives mentioned in the article
L. 212-3, in particular by evaluating the financial means necessary for the implementation of the plan. If the water development and management master plan has not identified areas for safeguarding strategic resources for the supply of drinking water within groundwater bodies and aquifers provided for in 3° of II of the article
L. 212-1, the sustainable development and management plan for water resources and aquatic environments identifies these zones. This plan can also: 1° Identify the areas referred to in 5° of II of the article
L. 211-3; 2° Establish an inventory of hydraulic works likely to significantly disturb aquatic environments and plan actions to improve the transport of sediments and reduce the siltation of watercourses and canals, taking into account economic uses of these works; 3° Identify, within the wetlands defined in 1° of I of the article
L. 211-1, strategic areas for water management whose preservation or restoration contributes to the achievement of the objectives referred to in IV of the article
